1.       Please provide an executive summary describing the nature and scope of the proposal.
Additionally, please include the following: (1) identification of all parties, (2) description of the
applicant and it’s licensure track record, (3) the type of transaction proposed including description
of the transaction and relevant costs, (4) summary of all transfer documents, (5) summary of the
organizational structure of the applicant and its affiliates, and (6) whether the facility will be
accredited (e.g. TJC, CHAP, etc.).


                                         THE FACILITY

       Holiday Operator, LLC d/b/a Holiday Rehabilitation and Healthcare Center (“Holiday
Center” or “Applicant”) is applying to become the new operator of the skilled nursing facility
known as Holiday Retirement Home, Inc. located at 30 Sayles Hill Road, Manville, Rhode Island
(the “Facility”).

        The Facility is licensed for 170 beds and offers short-term rehabilitative care, long-term
care, and memory care programs. In addition, through service agreements with local hospice
companies the Facility makes available hospice and respite services. Under these agreements, and
subject to the Facility’s admission policies and procedures, the Facility provides room and board
to qualifying hospice patients, as well as limited services, including the following: assistance with
activities of daily living; food and dietary supplements, as well as laundry and personal care
supplies; contacting family/legal representatives for purposes unrelated to the patient’s terminal
condition; and providing customary room furnishings. Services provided by hospice include, but
are not limited to, the following: medical social services provided by qualified social workers
under the direction of a physician; physician services; counseling services, including bereavement,
dietary and spiritual counseling; physical, respiratory, occupational and speech therapy services
provision of medical supplies, drugs and biologics; and medical direction and management,
including comprehensive care planning developed by an Interdisciplinary Group. Respite care
services include daily home health services furnished to hospice patients in order to provide relief
to family members for a period of five (5) or less consecutive days, or, on an intermittent, non-
routine and occasional basis.

      IDENTIFICATION OF THE PARTIES AND DESCRIPTION OF PROPOSED
                             TRANSACTION

        The Holiday Retirement Home, Inc. (“Holiday Retirement” or the “Seller”) is the
licensed operator of the Facility. Holiday Retirement also owns the real property on which the
Facility is located, situated at 30 Sayles Hill Road, Manville, Rhode Island (the “Property”). See
Tab 15 for pre-transfer organizational chart.




                                               Page 1

===== PAGE 5 =====
         Pursuant to the Asset Purchase Agreement (attached as Tab 14), Holiday Property, LLC
(“Holiday Property”), having the same ownership as Applicant, seeks to purchase the Property
where the Facility is located at, 30 Sayles Hill Road, Manville, Rhode Island, as well as operating
assets of the Facility for the purchase price of Sixteen Million One Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars
($16,150,000.00) (the “Purchase Price”). Holiday Property will then lease the Facility to its
affiliated company, Holiday Operator LLC, the Applicant. (See Proposed Lease Agreement at Tab
24).

        Post-closing, Applicant, by and through its affiliated entities, intends to invest approximately
Five Million One Hundred Thousand Dollars ($5,100,000.00) for ongoing capital improvements to
the Facility, which will focus on much needed updates and repairs, such as the HVAC system,
exterior of the Facility, resident rooms, lobby/entrance, nurses' stations, corridors, lounges and other
common areas, to create a more modern, home-like environment for residents, as well as enhancing
the Facility’s therapy gym, rehab capabilities and equipment (collectively the “Capital
Improvements”). The Purchase Price and Capital Improvements will be funded with 80% financing
and 20% equity.

                                  APPLICANT’S EXPERIENCE

         Applicant and its affiliates have a proven history of providing high-quality, long-term, care
services to their nursing facility residents. The Applicant, through its affiliates, collectively owns
eighty-one (81) skilled nursing and assisted living facilities in eight (8) states: Maryland,
Massachusetts, New Hampshire, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Pennsylvania, Florida and Virginia.
A full list of the affiliated facilities is provided in Tab G1. All of the facilities associated with the
Applicant are in compliance with the licensing agencies in their respective states. Several of
Applicant Owners’ skilled nursing facilities have received four and five-star ratings in U.S. News
& World Report “Best Nursing Homes” list. The Applicant, by and through its affiliates, have an
unparalleled track record of purchasing and managing skilled nursing facilities across the county.
The Applicant’s affiliates are experienced with rehabilitating insolvent and mismanaged nursing
facilities, specifically, improving operating efficiency, property conditions, and patient care. The
Applicant, by and through its affiliates, have developed and implemented a quality assurance and
performance improvement plan that has been proven to be effective in delivering and ensuring
quality patient care and facility performance.

   As discussed further, below, the Applicant and its affiliates are committed to ensuring the long
term success of the Facility and are prepared to invest millions of dollars into improving the
Facility, understanding that investment to be necessary to achieve its potential. The Applicant’s
business model is to purchase underperforming facilities with deferred capital expenses and
operational deficits. They then invest the necessary capital to make these facilities safer and more
comfortable for the residents and to improve the quality of care. They leverage high quality
relationships with local providers and a professional admissions team to increase facility census
and improve outcomes. As they have in their 81 other facilities, the Applicant and its affiliates are
confident that these investments will stabilize the financial condition of this struggling Facility and
position it for long-term success.



                                                Page 2

===== PAGE 6 =====
   The Applicant is independently operated and managed by an experienced facility-employed
administrator. Applicant intends to maintain consulting agreements with Marquis Health
Consulting Services (“Marquis Health”), Clinical Care Consulting (“Clinical Care”)
and Cedarbridge Financial Services (“Cedarbridge”) (See attached as Tab 9). Marquis Health
shall provide assistance with administrative services to the Facility, such as policy and
procedure development and marketing services. Clinical Care shall provide assistance with
clinical planning and strategy. Cedarbridge shall provide assistance with back office, billing,
payroll and account receivable services. Neither Marquis, Clinical Care nor Cedarbridge shall
serve in any capacity as operator or manager of Holiday Center. They also do not have any
authority to hire or fire the Facility’s chief executive officer, maintain or control the books
and records of the Facility, dispose of assets or incur liabilities on behalf of the Facility, or
adopt or enforce policies regarding the operation of the Facility.

It is the policy of the Applicant and its affiliates not to discriminate on the basis of race, creed,
color, religion, national origin, sex, gender, handicap, disability, blindness, source of
sponsorship, source of payment, marital status, age, sexual preference, genetic disposition, or
carrier status in the admission, retention and care of residents and patients. All persons and
organizations that have

occasion to either refer prospective residents or patients to Holiday Center are advised to do
so without regard to the person’s race, creed, color, religion, national origin, sex, gender,
handicap, disability, blindness, source of sponsorship, source of payment, marital status, age,
sexual preference, genetic disposition, or carrier status. This practice has been encouraged and
enforced by the Applicant’s affiliates with respect to all facilities they have an ownership
interest in.

Applicant intends to continue to provide access to traditionally underserved populations, as
evidenced by its Medicaid population.

21.      Please identify any planned actions of the applicant to reduce, limit, or contain health care
costs and improve the efficiency with which health care services are delivered to the citizens of
this state.

The Applicant will implement clinically guided programs and services to reduce
hospitalizations or unplanned discharges by working closely with physicians and acute care
partners. The Applicant, by and through its affiliates, have a history of improving efficiency
of its facilities across the country, which reduces and limits heath care costs to patients. The
Applicant intends to employ a specialized electronic system with respect to medical records,
as well as specialty clinical programs, along with telehealth to help reduce healthcare costs
and improve patient care. In addition, the Applicant will implement Urgent SNF for patients.
This provides an alternative option to individuals who do not meet acute admissions criteria
but require and/or will benefit from supervisory skilled nursing oversight or stabilization.
These services are provided at a discounted rate for up to 14 days. This rate is all inclusive
of room/board, meals, therapy and nursing services.

22.    Please provide a copy of the Quality Assurance Policies (for the services) and a detailed
explanation of how quality assurance for patient services will be implemented at the facility/ies by
the applicant.

The Applicant will maintain a robust Quality Assurance program. See Tab 22 for the
Quality Assurance Policies. As detailed with the Quality Assurance Policies, the Quality
Assurance and Performance Improvement Program (QAPI Program) will be implemented
by systems and tools developed and maintained by the Applicant’s affiliates, and monitored
by a QAPI committee, as well as ensuring proper staffing and training for employees and
staff.

23. Please provide a detailed description about the amount and source of the equity and debt
commitment for this transaction. (NOTE: If debt is contemplated as part of the financing, please
complete Appendix E). Additionally, please demonstrate the following:

  A.   The immediate and long-term financial feasibility of the proposed financing plan;

   The proposed transaction, the Purchase Price of $16,150,000, in addition to Capital
   Improvements, will be funded with a mix of equity contributions (20%) and debt
   (80%). Holiday Property intends to obtain a term loan from Forbright Bank (“Forbright”),
   which will be secured by a mortgage on the real property, as well as guarantees of Applicant
   and affiliated entity owners. The term loan will support eighty percent (80%) for the
   purchase of the Facility, as well the Capital Improvements, as evidenced by that Loan
   Commitment from Forbright provided in Tab 23. In addition, with respect to the 20%


                                               Page 7

===== PAGE 11 =====
   equity contribution, Applicant maintains substantial liquidity, through its majority owner,
   Quinto Nexgen, LLC, as evidenced by that letter provided by JP Morgan Bank, as included
   in Tab 23. Moreover, Quinto Nexgen, LLC shall provide a Line of Credit to Applicant to
   support operating expenditures, which said Line of Credit Agreement is included in Tab
   23.

  B.   The relative availability of funds for capital and operating needs; and

       As noted above, the Applicant has secured financing for both the acquisition of the
       Property and Capital Improvements of the Facility. The Applicant has obtained a Line
       of Credit from Quinto Nexgen LLC, its affiliate owner, which maintains substantial
       liquidity, to support operating expenditures and the projected losses during the
       stabilization period. The financing details are attached as Tab 23.


   C. The applicant’s financial capability.

       The Applicant’s financial capacity is documented in the financial statements included
       at Tab 23. Quinto Nexgen LLC, the majority owner of the Holdco, has a portfolio that
       includes sixteen nursing home facilities across the country; Holiday Center will be its
       seventeenth acquisition. Quinto Nexgen LLC’s diverse portfolio includes both the
       operator and real estate components. Quinto Nexgen LLC maintains sufficient liquidity
       to reflect its proportional ownership and equity contribution, to ensure Applicant’s
       financial solvency.

                                           Appendix C (CONT.)

5.      Based on the format below, please provide a summary of the applicant’s administrative and operational
policies and procedures to provide individualized and resident-centered care, services, and accommodations, and a
sense of peace, safety, and community, and clearly identify how the proposal would advance these areas:

a.       Resident’s physical environment:
      i. Accommodations for privacy vs. congregate and common areas;
         The Facility ensures that residents have privacy: Private, single-occupancy rooms, as well as
         semi-private rooms are offered. The Facility provides both common area day rooms and
         dining rooms to allow for socialization, communal activities, and resident gatherings.

     ii. Choice and autonomy in personal space, fixtures, furniture;
         Residents may furnish their room with their own furniture to the extent space and fire safety
         allows. Residents are also encouraged to decorate their room with personal belongings.

    iii. Access to and involvement in decentralized services, such as, community kitchen(s), laundry,
activities;
         Each resident neighborhood has a kitchenette for resident and family use. The Facility
         provides laundry services and has a beauty salon on site. The Facility also has a number of
         activities for residents each day. The schedule of daily activities is posted in common areas
         and provided to each resident. These activities include, fitness classes, games such as trivia,
         cards, or bingo, and gardening. The facility also makes available books, newspapers, games,
         and movies for independent resident use.

     iv. Access to outdoors and outdoor activities (e.g., sunrooms, patios, gardens and gardening);
         Residents may enjoy being outdoors independently and in organized activities. The Facility’s
         location in North Kingstown, near Narragansett Bay, allows for the residents to have
         numerous opportunities to enjoy the outdoors.

b.       Resident-centered systems of care:
       i.Security systems and care delivery systems to foster autonomy, choice, and negotiated risk;

        The facility has a security system preventing those at risk for wandering from exiting the
        facility without staff knowledge. This provides both for the safety of residents and for
        autonomy of residents who are safe to exit.                                             A
        CCTV system is installed in common areas and entrances and exits to allow for observation
        for safety and security purposes without limiting movement. A resident call system alerts
        staff via pagers when residents call for assistance. This provides an immediate notification
        to the resident’s caregiver of the need for help.

      ii.Individualized daily/nightly scheduling (e.g., daily rhythm, going to bed, waking);

        Residents are interviewed to identify their preferences which are incorporated into the
        plan of care. Care preferences include sleep and wake times, bath/shower schedules,
        activity preferences, etc. Should a resident be unable to provide information regarding



                                               Tab C – Page 4

===== PAGE 31 =====
       individualized preferences, family members and resident representatives are queried for
       the information. This information is included in the resident care plan, communicated to
       the entire team, reviewed with each care plan meeting, and updating accordingly.

     iii.Dining flexibility (e.g., time, access to dining style and menu choice);

       Daily menus include regular and alternate meals along with always available choices.
       Residents are queried for likes/dislikes regarding food preferences. Family members and
       resident representatives are involved for residents unable to respond. Information regarding
       meal preferences is incorporated into the care plans and reviewed with each care plan
       meeting and updated accordingly. The Facility makes a number of options available for
       individuals with dietary restrictions.

     iv.Lifestyle/activities flexibility;

       As set forth above, activities are resident-centered with an array of events and opportunities
       available at any given time for resident engagement.

c.     Workforce administration:

       i.How do staffing schedules and assignments ensure consistent delivery of resident services and foster
relationship building?

       The Applicant supports the Consistent Assignment model of care. Consistent Assignment
       allows for relationships to form between resident and caregiver and for the rhythms of life to
       be learned. The Interact Stop and Watch program is most effective when staffing with
       Consistent Assignment and fall prevention, observation of weight loss, and maintenance of
       skin integrity are all more effective with Consistent Assignment. Resident Life Stories tell
       the life story of the resident to caregivers allowing for more meaningful engagement between
       residents and caregivers and the support of a more meaningful and engaging activities
       program for each resident.

     ii.Administrative status strategies for dealing with licensed staff turn-over (e.g. Registered nurses,
Licensed Practical nurses, Nursing Assistants)

       A robust employee retention program and a culture of recognition and respect is the key to
       minimizing turnover and thereby maintaining consistent care standards. The Applicant will
       maintain such a program. The longevity of the staff at the current facility is one of its
       strengths and a feature which the Applicant hopes to maintain in the future.

   As evidenced by the Forbright Commitment in Tab 23, the interest rate may fluctuate based upon
   the 30-day CME Term Secured Overnight Financing Rate (“SOFR”), a compounding average
   over a 30-day period, plus 2.60%, which resets monthly. At a minimum, debt financing will be
   subject to an interest rate of 3.00%. For instance, as of the date of this Application, the interest
   rate would be 6.97%. Pursuant to the Forbright Commitment, interest only payments will
   required for the first two years, thereafter, monthly installments of interest and principal
   payments, amortized based upon a 25-year mortgage.

                      ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES AGREEMENT

         This Administrative Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is effective as of the ___ day of
_____________, 2025, by and between HOLIDAY OPERATOR LLC, a Rhode Island limited
liability company (“Licensed Operator”) licensed to operate a nursing home in Rhode Island
known as HOLIDAY REHABILITATION AND HEALTHCARE CENTER ("Facility"),
and MARQUIS HEALTH CONSULTING SERVICES LLC, a New Jersey Limited liability
company (the “Administrative Services Company”), (collectively the “Parties”).

       The Parties acknowledge receipt of valid consideration including the mutual promises
contained herein. Intending to be legally bound the Parties agree as follows:

I. Recitals

       A. The Licensed Operator is passionate about caring for residents and desires to provide
          high quality resident care and focus on clinical issues.
       B. The Licensed Nursing Home Administrator (the “Licensed Administrator”) manages
          all functions of the Licensed Operator and in many cases, such as for Nursing,
          Restorative Therapy, and Social Services Departments has licensed Clinicians who
          lead those departments under his or her guidance and direction.
       C. The Licensed Operator has other Department heads who are likewise managed by the
          Licensed Administrator for activities, physical plant, and the like, who have experience
          and expertise in their specific areas of operations.
       D. The Licensed Operator, the Licensed Administrator and the Department Heads have a
          multitude of resources available to them including the people who work at the Facility
          and in their departments as well as dozens of vendors and consultants.
       E. The Licensed Operator has a business office which works with financial service
          companies and accountants to bill, collect receivables, pay payable, keep books and
          records and to perform other back-office business related functions.
       F. The Licensed Operator desires for a consultant to assist it with organizational structure
          and more effectively utilize some of the foregoing resources as set forth herein.
       G. The Licensed Operator shall control and remain responsible for all matters relating to
          its day-to-day operations and the operation of the Facility.

       WHEREAS the Parties agree as follows:

II.    Relationship between the Parties

       A. The Licensed Operator has appointed and shall employ the Licensed Administrator
          who is responsible for management of the Facility.
       B. The Licensed Administrator is and shall remain, without regard to any input or
          contribution from the Administrative Services Company, solely responsible for
          ensuring: (i) the existence, adequacy, and proper implementation of all required and/or

===== PAGE 54 =====
   prudent policies and procedures; (ii) compliance with all rules, regulations, laws,
   governmental directives; (iii) compliance with all terms of its license(s) provider
   agreements and contracts; (iv) that it meets all applicable standards of care.



C. The Administrative Services Company is not in any way responsible for clinical, legal,
   accounting, tax or other services or advice that require specialized training, licensing
   and/or education. The foregoing is true even if the employees of the Administrative
   Services Company hold advanced and/or specialized degrees and/or certifications as
   the Administrative Service Company shall not provide professional advice and/or usurp
   the power, authority, or responsibility of those within or outside the facility who do
   offer professional advice or services.

D. The Licensed Operator shall maintain its independence as an operator, employer, and
   caregivers and does not hereby cede its independence or responsibilities to the
   Administrative Services Company in any way. Without limiting the generality of the
   foregoing, Licensed Operator shall make independent decisions concerning how to
   meet or exceed each and every duty of care and responsibility relating to: (i) residents,
   staff and visitors to the facility; (ii) governmental entities and authorities; (iii) any and
   all loans policy of insurance, and/or any other contract; (iv) employer/employee
   relationship and benefits; (v) workplace and/or safety; (vi) resident trust accounts, (vii)
   Payroll and/or withholding, (viii) any other responsibility it has as an owner, operator,
   tenant, lessee, borrower, employer, holder of any license and/or provider agreement
   and/or healthcare provider.
E. The Licensed Operator shall make all final decisions relating to: (i) patient/resident
   care, (ii) staffing, (iii) resources, (iv) billing, (v) admissions, (vi) discharge, (vii)
   budgets and in all ways remain responsible for the adoption and implementation of
   those decisions.
F. Without regard to its actions or inactions, the Administrative Services Company is
   hereby assuming no liability for any matters consisting of or relating to the areas set
   forth in Paragraphs II(A-E).

G. The Administrative Services Company may share non-protected external information
   and/or data with the Licensed Operator that it obtains from other customers and/or
   industry sources. The Licensed Operator hereby consents to permitting the
   Administrative Services Company to share non-protected information and/or data with
   other of its clients and/or industry sources to the extent it obtains the same from the
   Facility. The purpose of sharing information and data with others is to permit the
   Licensed Operators to have access to real time information that is greater than itself.



                                           2

===== PAGE 55 =====
H. The Licensed Operator hereby agrees that it shall not misuse this information and/or
   data. Licensed Operator acknowledges that every facility has different: (i) physical
   characteristics, (ii) residents, staff and community demographics, (iii) residents’
   diagnosis and acuity, (iv) leadership, (v) case mix , (vi) payor mix, (viii) marketplace,
   (ix) vendors, consultants and other external support, and (vii) is different in a multitude
   of other ways. Under no circumstances shall any comparative data be interpreted as an
   “expectation” to conform to the norm or to otherwise have the Facility attempt to
   change its processes and/or outcomes as a result of receipt of any comparative data.
   Comparative data may be used to spur discussions and thought.
I. The Administrative Services Company may likewise provide the Licensed Operator
   with ways to view its own real time and comparative data and/or assist the Licensed
   Operator to obtain a vendor who has the ability to do the same. The use of the foregoing
   may vary but again, care must be driven by the needs of each individual resident and
   not reports. Reports however may be used to spur discussion concerning why outcome
   is different than predictive models or why trends or statistical anomalies exist.
   Sometimes reporting may compare actual to budgeted numbers. Budgets are merely
   expectations based upon predictive analysis. When the predictive analysis and reality
   materially deviate it is an opportunity for discussion of process, and/or any need to
   modify the predictive analysis for the future. In no way shall resident care be changed
   solely because it does or does not conform to any budget or other predictive analysis.
J. The Facility warrants that care will, without regard to advice and/or suggestions from
   the Administrative Services Company, be driven by patient’s needs and governmental
   requirements. While the Administrative Services Company may be involved in
   preparing or distributing predictive analysis, it is not responsible for the clinical
   programs or care at the Facility or directing the Clinical staff.
K. The Licensed Operator shall be solely responsible for ensuring that each and every
   person and/or vendor of the facility that comes into contact with comparative data
   understands the foregoing and does not misconstrue the supply of comparative data or
   non-clinical advice as pressure to conform and does not change its conduct solely as a
   result of where the Facility’s numbers sit with regard to others or against its own
   predictive or historical data.. Subject to the foregoing, the data may be used or
   disregarded by the Licensed Operator in any way it desires and will be without liability
   to the Administrative Services Company.
L. Every communication from the Administrative Services Company or any of its officers,
   directors, employees, agents, servants, independent contractors or the like shall be
   deemed to have the following qualifiers “WITHOUT REGARD TO THIS
   COMMUNICATION: DO NOT DO ANYTHING IN CONTRAVENTION OF THE
   LAW; DO NOT ACT IN VIOLATION OF YOUR LEGAL OR CIVIL
   OBLIGATIONS; AND DO NOT ACT UNETHICALLY.”



                                          3

===== PAGE 56 =====
M. By entering this Agreement, the Parties intend for the Licensed Operator, and not the
   Administrative Services Company, to have responsibility for ensuring legality and/or
   propriety of actions taken or inactions of the Licensed Operator.
N. The Administrative Service Provider shall identify, assist in negotiations with, and
   work with other vendors. The Administrative Services Company shall not, as a result
   of the foregoing or otherwise be responsible for the actions or inactions of any other
   vendor or service provider.
O. The Licensed Operator is solely responsible for the implementation and approval of
   any and all budgets. Any role the Administrative Services Company is ministerial
   and/or advisory and is done with the express acknowledgement that the same is without
   liability to the Administrative Service Company.

P. The Administrative Services Company is not and shall not be deemed a manager,
   responsible party, controlling party, employee, officer, partner, principal, associate,
   joint employer, joint ventures, or affiliate of the Licensed Operator. The
   Administrative Services Company is and shall act and perform as an independent
   contractor to provide administrative and/or advisory services within the scope of the
   authority conferred by this Agreement. Consistent with the foregoing, nothing herein
   shall be construed as creating a partnership, joint venture, franchise, employment, co-
   employer or any other type of relationship other than limited service vendor which is
   an independent contractor of the Licensed Operator. Likewise, nothing in this
   Agreement shall be construed to change the employment relationship of any employee
   of the Licensed Operator.

Q. The Parties acknowledge that most of the tasks performed by the Administrative
   Services Company will be performed by persons who are not physically located at the
   facility.

R. The parties do not intend for any employee of Licensed Operator, the patients and/or
   residents and/or any third party to have privity of contract with the Administrative
   Services Company or hereby to confer any duty of the Administrative Services
   Company to any third party. There are no intended third-party beneficiaries to this
   Agreement and by virtue of executing this Agreement; the Administrative Services
   Company does not become an “operator” as defined by any applicable law or accept
   any responsibility concerning residents.

S. For the purposes of any restriction on liability contained in this Article II, the
   Administrative Services Company shall be deemed to include not just the
   Administrative Services Company but its parents, subsidiaries, members,
   shareholders and/or other owners, officers, directors, legal counsel, accountants,


                                        4

===== PAGE 57 =====
          employees, independent contractors, servants, representatives and/or agents of the
          same.




III.   Administrative Services

       A. VENDORS: During the Term of this Agreement, the Administrative Services
          Company shall assist the Facility in identifying and negotiating rates with vendors,
          professionals, lenders, and/or insurers as requested.
       B. LIAISON: During the Term of this Agreement the Administrative Service Company,
          to the extent requested, will act as a liaison to assist in maintaining relationships,
          communications and providing administrative support in relation to:
          a. Lease/Landlord(s) Tenant relationship;
          b. Loan Agreements/Lender(s) Borrower relationship;
          c. Contract of Insurance/Insurance Insured relationship;
          d. Information Technology Support Services;
          e. Financial, Legal, and Accounting Services and other vendors and service providers
              as requested;
          f. Department of State and the Department of Health relating to corporate formalities,
              licensure and permits renewals, administrative filings and the like; and
          g. Governmental Audits and/or Reviews as it relates to non-clinical issues.
.

       C. CONSULTING: During the Term of this Agreement the Administrative Services
          Company shall assist the Facility as a consultant concerning the following, but only to
          the extent clinical judgment is not required:
          a. Observe and help to improve, and coordinate as requested, the administrative
              functioning of the Facility (The Administrative Services Company will only be on
              site periodically, when it is on site, it is encouraged to send representatives to any
              and all Facility meetings so the Facility Services Company can familiarize itself
              with the administrative structure of the Facility and identify opportunities for
              improvement or otherwise assist with non-clinical issues.)
          b. Cooperate with Legal Counsel for the Licensed Operator and through Legal
              Counsel coordinate legal matters relating to the Facility;
          c. Cooperate with the Financial Service vendors, Business Office and help coordinate
              the efforts of those who provide financial services, and other services as requested,
              relating to the Facility;
          d. Identify Potential Vendors as Facility deems necessary or desirable and as
              requested negotiate contracts and/or termination of contracts with such vendors;

                                                 5

===== PAGE 58 =====
   e. Marketing and advice concerning media, website and social media (It is expressly
      understood that the Facility may employ a Public Relations Firm or others to assist
      in communication in which case the Administrative Services company shall act as
      a coordinator);
   f. Expanding or contracting programs or services (subject to all review deemed
      necessary or appropriate by the Licensed Operator including without limitation
      clinical and other review to ensure suitability—the assessment of which is not the
      responsibility of the Administrative Services Company);
   g. Long term Physical Plant improvements and major repairs;
   h. Employee Relations, Recruiting and/or Retention;
   i. Document Retention and Storage;
   j. Data Collection. Assisting in coordinating and/or creating vehicles for review and
      reporting without warranty that the same will be suitable for the specific use or
      sufficient for any regulatory purpose;
   k. General Customer Service and Satisfaction;
   l. Evaluation of the Licensed Administrator and the filling vacancies as needed;
   m. Cooperate with and assist as requested with the risk management, compliance, and
      quality assurance efforts;
   n. Assist in ensuring the Licensed Administrator is presented with formats and
      historical and trend data for budgets and provided other support as requested. (The
      Licensed Operator, through its Licensed Administrator shall set and approve and
      be solely responsible for the content and sufficiency of such budgets); and
   o. Other administrative support as requested.


D. QAPI: The Administrative Services company shall provide a representative to sit on
   the QAPI Committee, as and when such representative is available, to provide
   administrative service support and consulting services to the QAPI Committee as it
   does for the Licensed Operator more generally. To          the extent the Facility QAPI
   Committee desires the assistance of the Administrative Services Company with regard
   to any matter, the Administrative Services Company shall fully cooperate. All
   information conveyed to or from the QAPI Committee discussed within the QAPI
   Committee shall be kept confidential to the fullest extent permitted by law as it may be
   subject to the Quality Assurance and/or Peer Review or other privilege.

E. Any such advice and/or policies and procedures are provided without warranty or
   liability of any sort as the Licensed Operator is solely responsible for ensuring that
   financial decisions, clinical decisions, and all policies and procedures comply with all
   laws and all of its responsibilities to residents, the government, all payors etc….
   Without regard to when, how or in what context any financial, clinical and/or policy or


                                         6

===== PAGE 59 =====
             procedure material is tendered, the Licensed Operator shall independently review and
             assess the same before adopting, utilizing and/or implementing what is tendered or
             creating any conclusions or taking actions or not acting as a result.




IV.      Authority.

         A. To help the Administrative Services Company act as a liaison, the Licensed Operator
            hereby gives the Administrative Services Company authority to act in the capacity of
            “agent” for the Licensed Operator and as its Operator’s Representative in any dealings
            with governmental or quasi-governmental entities and all Intermediaries of the
            foregoing including without limitations Medicare, Medicaid, CMS, the Department of
            Health, and any other Federal, State, Municipal, other governmental or quasi-
            governmental entities which license, fund, regulate, or have oversight responsibility
            over the Licensed Operator, the business which it operates, or the assets which it
            possesses or more generally for the Medicare, Medicaid, or Tricare payment systems
            including without limiting the generality of the foregoing: The Office of the Attorney
            General, The Office of the Inspector General, The Department of Justice, Medicare,
            Medicaid, ZPIC, RAC, or MAC Contractors, the FBI, any Fraud Control Unit, the
            Department of Labor, the Food and Drug Administration, the Department of State, the
            Buildings Department etc. The Administrative Services Company will inform the
            Licensed Operator of any significant issues that may occur in its capacity as “agent”.
            The Licensed Operator may revoke this authority at any time upon written notice.

V.       Responsibility of Licensed Operator

      A. Licensed Operator shall not delegate to the Administrative Services Company and the
         Administrative Services Company will not be responsible for any powers and
         responsibilities not specifically allocated to the Administrative Services Company in this
         Agreement.

VIII.      Term.

      A. Unless sooner terminated pursuant hereto, the term of this Agreement begins on the
         date hereof and shall continue for one (1) year (the “Term”). The Agreement shall
         automatically be renewed from term to term.

      B. Termination for Cause: The Licensed Operator and/or the Administrative Services
         Company may terminate the Agreement for cause with thirty (30) days written notice,
         should: (i) the Licensed Operator of the facility cease to be the current licensed
         operator; or (ii) should the other party materially breach this agreement. In such case
         of termination for cause, the Licensed Operator shall be obligated for payment to the
         Administrative Services Company through the date of termination and neither party
         shall have any obligation to the other, except for any obligations to defend indemnify
         and/or hold the other harmless relating to the period after the date of termination.

      C. Termination without Cause. The Licensed Operator and/or the Administrative Services
         Company may terminate this Agreement without cause upon sixty (60) days prior
         written notice to Licensed Operator or to the Administrative Services Company as the
         case may be. In such case of termination without cause, the Licensed Operator shall
         be obligated for payment to the Administrative Services Company through the date of
         termination and neither party shall have any obligation to the other, except for any
         obligations to defend indemnify and/or hold the other harmless relating to the period
         after the date of termination.


IX.       Fee.

      A. As compensation for the services set forth herein, the Licensed Operator shall pay to
         the Administrative Services Company the fees as set forth on Schedule A (the “Fees”).
         The Fees shall be paid monthly.

      B. Notwithstanding the Fee schedule set forth on Schedule A, in the event that the Fees
         charged are less than the cost of services rendered, the Parties shall equitably increase
         the Fees to be no less than the Administrative Services Company’s cost of providing
         services to the Licensed Operator.

      C. In the event that the Licensed Operator fails to pay the foregoing Fee as and when due,
         the Administrative Services Company shall be entitled to all costs of collections from
         the Licensed Operator including without limiting the generality of the foregoing, all
         sheriff fees, collection company fees, attorney, paralegal, accounting and all other costs

                                          EXHIBIT A
                                       FEE SCHEDULE



                (a)    Marquis Health Consulting Services LLC will bill the Operator on or about
the 30th of each month for services rendered during said month. The Operator shall make payments
to Marquis Health Consulting Services LLC on or before the 15th of the month immediately
following the month in which the services were rendered. Marquis Health Consulting Services’
compensation shall be in the amount equal to six percent (6%) of the Operator’s Gross Revenues
(the “Fee”) for each month minus fees paid to vendors relating to (a) billing and collections
services, (b) “back office” accounting services and (c) clinical support and general oversight
services. Gross Revenue determined for any period shall be in accordance with generally accepted
accounting principles consistently applied, including but not limited to all collected revenues and
income of any kind derived directly from Facility operations (including collected rentals or other
payments from concessionaires, licenses, tenants, and other users of the Facility, but not gross
receipts of such concessionaires, licenses, tenants or uses), excluding:

               i.       Federal, state, and municipal excise, sales and use taxes, collected directly
                        from patients as a part of the sales price of any goods or services;
               ii.      Proceeds of any life insurance policies;
             iii.       Gains arising from the sale or other disposition of capital assets;
              iv.       Any reversal of any contingency or tax reserve;
               v.       Advances on the line of credit or other borrowed funds;
              vi.       Interest earnings from agency or other accounts; and
             vii.       Gifts of any kind or nature to the Operator.


To illustrate, Marquis Health Consulting Services LLC will bill for its services for the month of
January 2025, on or about January 30, 2025, and payment shall be due by February 15, 2025.

               (b)    The parties hereto agree that the compensation provided in this Section is
consistent with fair market value in an arm’s-length transaction and is not and has not been
determined in a manner that takes into account the volume or value of any referrals or business
otherwise generated for or with respect to Operator or between the parties or any of the respective
owners thereof for which payment may be made in whole or in part under Medicare or any state
health care program or under any other payor program.

                           CONSULTING SERVICES AGREEMENT

       THIS CONSULTING SERVICES AGREEMENT (this "Agreement"), dated as of
___________ ___, 2025 is by and between HOLIDAY OPERATOR LLC, a Rhode Island
limited liability company ( “Operator”) licensed to operate a nursing home in Rhode Island
known as Holiday Rehabilitation and Healthcare Center ("Facility") with an address of
30 Sayles Hill Road, Manville, Rhode Island 02838 and CLINICAL CARE
CONSULTANTS LLC a New Jersey limited liability company, having its principal place
of business at 1608 Route 88, Suite 301, Brick, NJ 08724 ("Consultant"). Operator and
Consultant may each be referred to as a "Party" and collectively as the "Parties".

         WHEREAS, Consultant provides limited consulting services for long term care
facilities in areas which require the understanding of clinical function;

       WHEREAS, Consultant does not own and is not an operator or nursing home administrator
or a Management Company and does not itself provide or direct clinical services;

        WHEREAS, Operator is and shall remain legally responsible for the operation of the
Facility and while maintaining responsibility and control;

       WHEREAS, Operator desires to retain Consultant and Consultant desires to provide
limited services as set forth and pursuant to the terms and conditions set forth herein;

       NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants and undertakings of
the Parties hereto, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency
of which are hereby acknowledged, the Parties hereto, intending to be legally bound, do
hereby agree as follows:

                                    ARTICLE 1
                             RELATIONSHIP OF THE PARTIES

1.     Retention of Authority by Operator.

         1.1 Appointment of Consultant. Operator hereby engages Consultant to provide
 certain clinical consulting services (the "Services"), as described in Article 2. Consultant
 accepts such appointment, pursuant to the terms and conditions contained herein.

         1.2 Third Party Beneficiaries. By virtue of entering into this Agreement or providing
 any services hereunder, Consultant does not create a duty to any third-parties, including but not
 limited to residents, and there are no intended third-party beneficiaries to this Agreement.
 Consultant’s only duty is to the Operator and the extent of that duty is fully set forth and limited
 by the terms of this Agreement.

       1.3 Compliance with Law. Without regard to any advice or directions from the
 Consultant, Operator at all times retains the obligation to act ethically and to provide all
 goods and services and to invoice and establish and implement policies and procedures

===== PAGE 70 =====
 which are consistent with all applicable Federal, State and local statutes, rules and
 regulations, including without limiting the generality of the foregoing, anti-kickback
 statutes, false claims acts, reverse false claims acts, HIPPA and other privacy statutes; Stark
 and other anti-referral legislation; the FLMA and other labor laws and employee and/or
 resident safety regulations and all other rules and laws. Neither the Consultant nor the
 Operator desires for the Facility to act in any way contrary to its responsibilities and
 notwithstanding anything contained herein to the contrary, the Operator is solely
 responsible for ensuring that all of its actions are consistent with all rules, regulations, laws
 and all required standards of conduct.

         1.4 Communications. The Operator understands that communication from the
 Consultant may sometimes be inartful, subject to misinterpretation, or not fully reflect the
 views of its author or the Consultants. In addition, for efficiency and to reduce the costs of
 the services provided hereunder, the Consultant does not intend to utilize legal Counsel to
 screen communication and instead relies upon the Facility to do so. To the extent that
 anything written or spoken by Consultant or anyone on its behalf, could be interpreted as
 illegal, immoral or improper, such communication must be discarded to the extent it is
 unethical, legally impermissible and/or to the extent the same is inconsistent with best
 practices. The Facility understands this obligation and accept the same.

         1.5 Retention of Ownership and Control. Operator holds and shall continue to
 hold all necessary licenses and permits to operate the Facility. The Nursing Home
 Administrator (the “Administrator”) is licensed to and shall manage the facility operations.
 Notwithstanding anything contained herein to the contrary, in no way does the Operator, the
 Licensed Nursing Home Administrator or any of the Department Heads herein cede its, or
 collectively their, responsibility for the operation to the Consultant nor does the Consultant
 accept such responsibility. Operator shall remain responsible for ensuring that any services
 provided pursuant to this Agreement complies with all pertinent provisions of Federal, State,
 and local laws, rules, regulations and ordinances, and standards of accreditation. Operator
 shall retain all ultimate authority for the overall policies, procedures, operation and assets of
 the Facility. All final decisions and all responsibility for ensuring the existence and
 implementation of adequate and appropriate policies and procedures and all responsibility
 for caring for residents in addition to responsibility for properly assessing, coding,
 documenting and all other aspects of providing and billing for Facility services shall be the
 sole responsibility of the Operator. It is expressly understood that Consultant’s role is merely
 suggestive and advisory and without liability. Operator shall not delegate to Consultant, and
 Consultant will not be responsible for, any powers and/or responsibilities not specifically
 allocated to Consultant in this Agreement. Without limiting the generality of the foregoing,
 Operator retains and shall not delegate authority and responsibility over the following:

               1.5.1 Patient Care;

               1.5.2        Hiring, terminating and setting of all rules relating to any of the Facility's
employees, including, but not limited to: key management employees, the Administrator, the Medical
Director, the Director of Nursing and members of the medical staff of the Facility;
                                                   2

===== PAGE 71 =====
                    1.5.3 The books and records of the Facility and/or Operator and all financial issues;

                    1.5.4 Disposing of assets and incurring liabilities on behalf of the Operator and/or the
 Facility;

                1.5.5 Adopting, approving and enforcing policies and procedures regarding the
 operation of the Facility and/or the care of residents;

                    1.5.6 Setting or modifying the operating and/or capital and/or other budgets relating
 to the Facility;

                    1.5.7 Billing and/or collection;

                    1.5.8 Compliance with rules, laws and regulations;

                    1.5.9 Day-to-day operations; and

                    1.5.10 Any other task which is not expressly delegated to the Consultant pursuant
 hereto.

        1.6     Status of Parties. Consultant is a non-exclusive independent contractor that engages
in the operation of its own business. Neither Party is, nor shall be considered, the agent or employee
of the other Party for any purpose. Neither Party has the authority to enter contracts or assume any
obligations for the other Party, nor to make any warranties or representations on behalf of the other
Party. Nothing in this Agreement is to be construed as establishing a relationship of co-partners and/or
joint venturers between the Parties. The Parties understand and agree that they are not co-employers
and no Party will withhold on behalf of any employee or agent of the other Party any sums for income
tax, unemployment insurance, social security, or any other withholding pursuant to any law or
requirement of any governmental body relating to the payment of employee benefits.

        1.7 Eligibility of Consultant. Neither Consultant nor its principals and/or its employees are
precluded from providing the services as described herein or are subject to disciplinary action by any
licensing board including without limitation suspension or revocation of a license. On a go forward
basis, consultant shall perform no less frequent than monthly, LEIE checks to determine it employee
and principal’s eligibility and shall immediately notify the Operator in the event of any change in
circumstances. Consultant shall be responsible to Operator for any fines and/or other penalties imposed
upon it in relation to the Consultant’s breach of this Article 1.7.


       .
                        CONSULTING AND ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES

  2.       Scope of Services. Consultant shall provide the following services:

          2.1 Assist the Operator customize its policies and procedures. It is
   understood that the Operator utilizes and intends to continue to utilize policies and
                                                       3

===== PAGE 72 =====
procedures which are purchased by it from a reputable third party. Operator is and
shall remain responsible for the adequacy of all policies and procedures and the
appropriate implementation and monitoring of the same.

         2.2 Assist the QAPI Committee. The Consultant shall assist the QAPI
committee as needed in its confidential and protected efforts to identify how the
Facility organization is performing, including assessing clinical data to help identify
where and why Facility performance is at risk or has failed to meet standards and
identifying areas of opportunity and testing new approaches to fix underlying causes
of persistent/systemic problems or barriers to improvement and continuously study
and improve processes with the intent to better services or outcomes and assist the
QAPI Committee to improve processes involved in healthcare delivery and resident
quality of life. It is understood that by virtue of Consultant’s support or involvement,
it is not responsible for the effectiveness of the QAPI Committee, which shall remain
the sole responsibility of the Operator. Unless otherwise required by law, Consultant
shall keep all matters discussed during the QAPI process confidential—except as to
other member of the QAPI Committee or those assisting the QAPI Committee. To
the extent asked to disclose the same, Consultant shall promptly advise the Operator
who, at Operator’s expense, shall be given the opportunity to assert any applicable
privilege, such as the quality assurance privilege.

        2.3 Periodically observe and report observations concerning the Dietary,
Physical Therapy, Social Services and Nursing Departments to the QAPI Committee
or representative thereof such as the Administrator or the applicable Department Head
to report to and be addressed by the QAPI Committee. Such observation may include
sitting in on any Facility meetings during which clinical matter are or may be
discussed. It is expressly understood that Consultant is not the manager of any of the
foregoing departments and it is not anticipated that the Consultant will observe most
issues, potential issues, or opportunities for improvement. Its job is only to offer
advice as it becomes aware of issues. Consultant shall not be held responsible for
failing to identify any of the foregoing and or failure to implement any
recommendation.

         2.4 Assist the Facility in connection with preparedness for governmental
surveys, inspections and/or investigations and responses to any adverse findings with
regard to the same. Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, the Consultant may
act as a liaison to the governmental representative(s), assisting the Facility in its attempts
to cooperate with governmental officials and ensuring the information provided is
complete and accurate, drafting of plans of corrections, assessing dates of compliance,
preparing IDRs or IIDRs and the formation of post survey, inspection, or investigation
recommendations, and working to support legal or other consultants. All work performed
in anticipation of governmental audit, investigation or litigation of any sort, and all
communication to or from legal counsel or concerning advice from counsel shall be held
in strict confidence and shall not be disclosed to third parties. Should anyone seek such
confidential information, Consultant shall immediately notify the Operator, so the
                                                 4

===== PAGE 73 =====
 Operator may at its expense, protect that which is subject to the Work Product Doctrine,
 the Attorney Client Privilege or is otherwise privileged.

         2.5 As requested, assist the Facility and/or its consultants, lawyers and/or insurers
 in performing risk management assessments and analysis. As these functions are typically
 in anticipation of litigation or to support defense of existing litigation, such work is to be
 held as confidential. Should anyone seek such confidential information, Consultant shall
 immediately notify the Operator, so the Operator may at its expense, protect that which is subject
 to the Work Product Doctrine, the Attorney Client Privilege or is otherwise privileged.

         2.6 Assist the Facility to develop controls and systems for honestly, ethically and
 appropriately assessing and recording resident’s condition and needs as the foregoing
 relates to Minimum Data Set (MDS) assessment data and if applicable, Case Mix Index.
 Notwithstanding the foregoing, Consultant shall not be responsible for accuracy of billing
 and the full responsibility for the same shall remain with the Operator.

         2.7 Make yourselves generally available to Facility Leadership and consultants as an
 educated and experienced resource to offer opinions, without warranty, on clinical and
 operational issues as they relate to the Facility.

                                       COMPENSATION
3.     Compensation

        3.1   Fees. In consideration for the Services provided by Consultant during the Term
of this Agreement, Operator shall pay to Consultant fees in an amount of $25,900 ("Service
Fee") per month, on the first day of each month following execution. Should the cost of
services exceed the fee, Consultant shall inform the Operator and Operator may at its
discretion pay the greater of the actual cost from the date of notice forward or cancel such
services.

        3.2    The Parties agree that the compensation provided herein is consistent with
fair market value in an arm's-length transaction and is not and has not been determined in a
manner that takes into account the volume or value of any referrals or business otherwise
generated for or with respect to the Facility or between the Parties or any of the respective
owners thereof for which payment may be made in whole or in part under Medicare or any
state health care program or under any other payor program.

4.     Term and Termination
                                                 5

===== PAGE 74 =====
       4.1    Term. The term of this Agreement will begin as of the date hereof (the
"Commencement Date") and will continue for an initial term of one (1) year (the "Term").
Thereafter, this Agreement will renew automatically for one (1) year periods (each, a
"Renewal Term") unless sooner terminated in accordance with the terms and conditions of
this Agreement.

        4.2    Termination. This Agreement may be terminated upon the occurrence of any
of the following events:

               4.2.1 Termination for Cause. This Agreement may be terminated by either
Party for cause. "Cause" shall mean a material breach of any provision, obligation, covenant,
representation or warranty set forth in this Agreement, which breach or violation is not cured
to the commercially reasonable satisfaction of the non-breaching Party within thirty (30) days
of its giving of detailed written notice of such material breach to the other Party.
Notwithstanding the foregoing, either Party may terminate this Agreement immediately if the
breaching Party does not, within seven (7) days, cure a failure by it to comply with a material
pertinent provision of any Federal, State or local statute, rule or regulation in carrying out its
responsibilities hereunder.

             4.2.2 Immediate Termination. This Agreement may be terminated
immediately by either Party in writing upon the following: disbarment of either Party from
the Medicare or Medicaid programs and, in the case of Consultant, disbarment of any of its
owners. Consultant shall immediately notify Operator in writing in the event any of its
employees that provide services to the Facility pursuant to this Agreement are excluded from
the Medicare and/or Medicaid programs.

              4.2.3 Without Cause. Each party may terminate this Agreement without
cause upon thirty (30) days prior written notice to the other party.

               4.2.4 Post Termination Actions. Upon termination, all work performed by
Consultant and all of its notes, data, information gathered, correspondence, calculations, and
material otherwise received, utilized or generated in the performance of the duties set forth
herein, shall be deemed the property of the Operator and shall be promptly tendered by the
Consultant to the Operator. Consultants shall not retain a copy of the foregoing. Without
limiting the generality of the foregoing, the Consultant shall return all work performed for the
Operator, pursuant to this Agreement and in its possession as a result of this Agreement or its
relationship with the Operator. The Consultant shall also refrain for one year after termination
from directly or indirectly hiring or soliciting on its own accord any for any other person, anyone
who worked for the Operator at the time of termination or within 6 months of such time.

                                            COVENANTS

5.      Nondisclosure of Proprietary Information; Nonsolicitation.

         5.1    The Parties acknowledge and agree that during the Term they will have
access to Confidential Information (as defined below) and other proprietary information
of the Parties as well as personal healthcare information of Residents and information
which is protected by one or more privileges such as the work product doctrine, the
attorney client privilege and or the quality assurance/peer review privilege. Neither Party
shall, nor shall its members, employees, representatives and agents, except as may be
required under applicable law or by any lawful subpoena, court order or legal process, at
any time without the other Party's prior written consent (i) disclose any such information
to any third party, or (ii) reproduce or utilize any such information in furtherance of any
other business venture. If a Party is required under applicable law or by lawful subpoena,
court order, or legal process to disclose any Confidential Information or other proprietary
information of the other, such disclosing party shall provide sufficient notice thereof to the
other to enable such party to seek a protective order or other appropriate legal or equitable
remedy to prevent such disclosure; provided that each party shall be permitted to disclose the
Confidential Information to its legal and other advisers. Furthermore, Consultant shall also
keep medical records of the Facility confidential in accordance with all applicable state and
Federal laws, including, but not limited to, Health Insurance Portability and Accountability
Act of 1996 ("HIPAA") and its related regulations and shall execute and abide by the Business
Associate Agreement, annexed hereto and incorporated herein as Exhibit "A". For purposes
of this Article 5, Section 5.1, the term "Confidential Information" shall mean the non-public
information of a Party, or any entity with which a Party contracts to provide any of the
Services, including, but not limited to, formulae, patterns, compilations, programs, devices,
methods, systems, techniques, processes, financial information, business strategies, costing
data, patient lists, payor lists, patient surveys, manuals, policies and procedures, forms, and
contractual arrangements and shall also include all potentially privileged information
including that which is potentially subject to the Work Product, Attorney Client, the QA or
other privilege . Confidential Information shall not include information which: (w) is known
to the receiving Party prior to receiving it from the other Party (except to the extent privileged);
(x) is generally known to the public; (y) is disclosed to one Party at any time by a third party
who had the legal right to disclose it; or (z) is independently developed by the other Party in
compliance with law. The provisions of this Article 5, Section 5.1 shall survive the termination
or assignment of this Agreement.
         5.2 Consultant shall not during the term of the Agreement or thereafter for a period of 12
months solicit any person who was employed by the Operator to work with or for the consultant or
hire such person directly or indirectly through another without regard to job title or salary. It is
agreed that this provision may be enforced by injunction. Consultant hereby consents to the entry
of an injunction and waives any, and all, requirements for Operator to post a bond. Likewise, in
lieu of actual damages, liquidated damages shall be imposed for all breaches of this provision and
all attorney fees and all cost of litigation relating to any injunction or damages shall be recoverable.
Liquidated damages shall be equal to one year salary for such individual as paid by Operator,
including all benefits, bonusses and the like using the annualized rate from the last pay increase.

                                         ARTICLE 6
                                         INSURANCE


         6.1 During the Term and any Renewal Term, Consultant shall obtain and maintain the
following insurance: malpractice or professional liability, general liability, automobile
liability, workers' compensation, employer's liability and special form coverage. The
malpractice or professional liability coverage shall contain limits of liability of not less than
One Million Dollars ($1,000,000.00) per occurrence and Three Million Dollars
($3,000,000.00) in the annual aggregate. General liability and automobile liability coverage
shall be in amounts not less than One Million Dollars ($1,000,000.00) per occurrence.
Workers' compensation and employer's liability coverage shall be in statutory amounts and in
compliance with the laws and regulations.

        6.2 On or before the Commencement Date (and thereafter upon request by one Party
to the other), each Party shall provide the other with certificates of insurance evidencing the
insurance required to be maintained under this Agreement. The certificates of insurance
required hereunder shall state that each Party shall be provided with thirty (30) days notice of
changes in carriers or material terms of the coverage. Each Party shall also arrange for
appropriate levels of coverage for all subcontractors, shall maintain in its files evidence of all
subcontractors' insurance coverage, and shall provide proof of such coverage to the other Party
upon request. Each Party agrees to maintain any insurance required hereunder for a period of
not less than three (3) years following the termination of this Agreement that is underwritten
on a claims made rather than on an occurrence basis, unless such continuing coverage is not
required in order for the Parties to maintain coverage with the limits identified above. The
terms of this Section shall not be deemed to limit the liability of the Parties, or to limit any
rights the Parties may have, including, without limitation, rights of indemnity and
contribution.

       6.3 Operator shall name Consultant as an additional insured on its general liability and
professional liability insurance policies.

        6.4 Each party shall defend, indemnify and hold the other party harmless from all
claims, liabilities, losses, fines and other expenses (including reasonable attorneys' fees)
arising from such party's negligent or intentional acts or omissions or the negligent or
intentional acts or omissions of such party's employees, shareholders, agents, representatives,
contractors or subcontractors. Consultant shall indemnify and hold harmless Operator to the
extent permitted by law from and against any and all liability, claims, and demands for all
payments required by any applicable unemployment and disability insurance, self-
employment taxes, social security, income tax and other withholdings, deductions, and
payments required by Federal, State or local laws, or otherwise authorized from or based upon
payments made to Consultant in connection with the provision of Services pursuant to this
Agreement.

                                           ARTICLE 7
                                    INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY

                      7.1 Any intellectual property developed by the Consultants in relation to the
    Facility is the exclusive property of the Operator on a buy-out basis. To the extent that intellectual
    property is jointly developed for the Operator and other clients and/or to the extent that a Court of
    Law or other finder of fact determines that any intellectual property utilized and/or created or
    developed by the Consultant and used in relation to the Facility in any way(s) is determined to be
    incapable of transfer to the Operator, the Consultant shall be deemed to have given the Operator
    an international license for unlimited royalty free use of such intellectual property with the
    Operator having all rights to sublicense to other individuals. Consultant shall cooperate in the
    physical and legal transfer of such intellectual property upon request and in the manner requested
    at its sole cost.

                         CONSULTING SERVICES AGREEMENT

      THIS SERVICES AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is made and entered into as of this
____ day of _______________, 2025 (the “Effective Date”) by and between HOLIDAY
OPERATOR LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company (“Licensee”), as licensed operator,
and CEDARBRIDGE FINANCIAL SERVICES LLC, a New Jersey limited liability company
(“Cedarbridge Service Consultant”).

                                           RECITALS

       WHEREAS, Licensee is the operator of a healthcare and rehabilitation facility located 30
Sayles Hill Road, Manville, Rhode Island 02838 to be known as HOLIDAY REHABILITATION
AND HEALTHCARE CENTER (the “Facility”).

        WHEREAS, Cedarbridge Service Consultant is engaged in the business of providing
financial services to long term care facilities with Licensee being solely responsible for the
operation of the Facility in compliance with applicable law;

        WHEREAS, Cedarbridge Service Consultant has agreed to provide Services (as defined
in Section 2 hereof) to Licensee upon the terms and subject to the conditions hereinafter set forth;
with Licensee being solely responsible for the operation of the Facility in compliance with
applicable law and

        WHEREAS, Licensee and Cedarbridge Service Consultant have agreed to enter into this
Agreement and Licensee hereby retains Cedarbridge Service Consultant on and subject to the
terms, conditions and provisions set forth herein.

                                         AGREEMENT

       NOW THEREFORE, for and in consideration of the mutual covenants, agreements and
promises, which the parties set forth below, and other good and valuable consideration, the receipt
and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the parties agree as follows:

1.     Term. The term of this Agreement (“Term”) shall commence when the Licensee begins
       operating at the Facility and shall continue until either party sends prior written notice to
       the other party of that party’s intent to terminate this Agreement sixty (60) days prior to
       the annual anniversary of the Effective Date unless the Term is sooner terminated pursuant
       to the terms of the Agreement.

2.     Duties. Cedarbridge Service Consultant shall perform the following services (the
       “Services”):

       (a)     Provide, in coordination with Licensee’s office staff, bookkeeping, accounting and
               reporting services for the Facility pertaining to all of its accounts receivable
               (including without limitation all private pay residents, Medicare residents, and
               Medicaid residents) and accounts payable, including but not limited to, routine
               billing of its accounts and assisting in the regular or periodic performance of the
               Facility’s cash flow analysis;

===== PAGE 83 =====
(b)   When necessary, at Licensee’s cost, secure the services of additional bookkeepers
      and contract accountants to prepare and review the Facility’s financial records and
      consult with and advise the Licensee and its agents regarding their timely
      preparation, content and submittals;

(c)   Assist with and provide for the timely preparing and filing of any and all financial
      records and/or reports (including but not limited to Medicaid and/or Medicare cost
      reports) relating to the Facility’s operation, and also financial plans to be reviewed
      and approved by Licensee on a periodic basis, including but not limited to: (a) an
      estimated profit and loss statement, in reasonable detail, for the current operating
      year; and (b) estimates for such operating year, covering expenditures for: (i)
      repairs and maintenance; (ii) the purchase of operating equipment; (iii) the purchase
      of furnishings and equipment; (iv) capital improvements; and (v) advertising and
      business promotion costs of the Facility;

(d)   Provide for the retention of and the appropriate monitoring of independent
      accountants and attorneys to timely prepare and file any and all required state and
      federal tax returns, appeals, etc.;

(e)   Perform on behalf of the Facility and jointly with the other facilities with whom
      Cedarbridge Service Consultant contracts from time to time, group purchasing and
      procurement of medical equipment, appliances, supplies, food and other items, as
      directed by the Licensee;

(f)   Provide or arrange with third parties, at Licensee’s cost, to provide all payroll
      functions including all regulatory and tax reporting functions relating to payroll
      administration.

(g)   Assist Licensee in establishing and maintaining banking relationships, including
      securing loans on behalf of Licensee, complying with the loan terms, supplying
      banks with any and all necessary documentation to properly document and support
      loan levels and otherwise manage Licensee’s cash flow and cash management
      needs, to the extent of Licensee’s cash availability. As part of its cash management
      services, Cedarbridge Service Consultant may, with the consent of Licensee and
      other facilities, arrange for and process loans between Licensee and its affiliates in
      a manner that minimizes the interest and financing costs to Licensee and allows for
      the orderly cash management of funds for Licensee. Cedarbridge Service
      Consultant shall properly document any such loans and shall implement same only
      with the written consent of Licensee and such other facilities. Nothing contained
      herein shall require Cedarbridge Service Consultant to advance any sums to or for
      the benefit of Licensee;

(h)   Establish bank accounts in Licensee’s name for the Facility at banking or other
      financial institution(s) reasonably acceptable to Licensee. The Cedarbridge Service
      Consultant shall deposit in such accounts all monies received in the name of
      Licensee from the operation of the Facility and will disburse the same for the
      purposes set forth in (k) below with signature authority over bank accounts to which

===== PAGE 84 =====
            governmental accounts receivable shall be deposited being vested solely in
            Licensee;

     (i)    From the accounts referred to in (j) above or from other monies of Licensee, pay
            on behalf of, and in the name of Licensee, such amounts and at such times as are
            required in connection with the leasing, operation, management or maintenance of
            the Facility;

     (j)    Cedarbridge Service Consultant is empowered by the Licensee and authorized to
            act as its agent to negotiate rates of reimbursement from any and all applicable
            governmental agencies and non-governmental entities, and shall provide all books,
            records, and other financial information or any related audits thereof;

     (k)    Cedarbridge Service Consultant may, assist the Licensee – or its billing agency to,
            institute any and all actions or proceedings to collect charges or other income due
            to the Facility and to identify and make refunds or submit corrected claims to third
            party payers and residents as required by applicable law or contract, disclosures
            relating to claims, responses to additional development requests, payer audits,
            recoupments including pursuing appeals therefrom, as provided however, Licensee
            is responsible for the submission of all claims for reimbursement including without
            limitation compliance with all conditions of participation, conditions of payment,
            medical necessity requirements, documentation requirements, coverage
            requirements, or other billing requirements.

     (l)    Assist Licensee in preparing, maintaining and updating Licensees licensing and
            provider enrollment and relating filings.

     (m)    Assist Licensee on the ongoing maintenance, development and improvement of the
            Information Technology system to appropriately oversee confidential Facility
            functions including banking, treasury, cash management, risk management,

            patient/employee data, NJS transmissions, attorney-client work products, etc.;

     (n)    Retain outside legal counsel to perform all legal services for Licensee; and

     (o)    Cedarbridge Service Consultant will make itself available to assist, advise and
            perform such other duties as may reasonably be requested by Licensee.

     (a)   The parties agree that Licensee is, and at all times during the Term hereof shall

           against findings, deficiencies and violations issued by applicable state, federal and
           regulatory bodies and Cedarbridge Service Consultant shall have no responsibility
           with respect thereto.

     (c)   Licensee represents and warrants that it and its respective personnel: (i) are, and

                THIS ASSET PURCHASE AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is made and entered into
