        Home, Inc., a Rhode Island business corporation (the “Real Property Seller” and “Old
        Operator”, as applicable, collectively, the “Seller”) having an address of 30 Sayles Hill Road,
        Manville, Rhode Island 02838, and Holiday Property LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability

        “Purchaser,”).

        Rehabilitation Center (the “Facility”) and

        herein) and the Facility upon the terms and conditions set forth in this Agreement.

        agreements set forth herein, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged by the

        agrees to purchase from Real Property Seller, upon the terms and conditions hereinafter set forth,

Purchaser shall deposit an additional Two Hundred Fifty Thousand ($250,000.00) Dollars with the
Escrow Agent (the “Additional Deposit”) which shall become part of the Deposit (the Initial
Deposit, the Additional Deposit and the Extension Deposit, if any, shall collectively be referred to
as the “Deposit”). Any Extension Deposit shall be included in the term “Deposit” and any
disposition of the Deposit shall include the Extension Deposit. Except as otherwise set forth in
this Agreement, after the expiration of the Due Diligence Period, the Deposit shall be non-
refundable and shall be credited to the Purchase Price at Closing.

        6.      Due Diligence.

                a.      Purchaser shall have the period until the date which is thirty (30) days after
    the receipt of a fully executed Agreement from Seller and the delivery of the Due Diligence
    Materials (the “Due Diligence Period”) to inspect the Property and to review the Due
    Diligence Materials. At any time prior to the expiration of the Due Diligence Period, Purchaser
    may terminate this Agreement for any reason or for no reason and in such event the Deposit
    shall be delivered to Purchaser and the parties shall have no further obligations to each other.



                                                    4

===== PAGE 102 =====
               b.      Seller shall deliver the items in Seller’s possession and control through an
   agent as set forth in Seller’s due diligence list (the “Due Diligence Materials”) to the extent
   not previously delivered prior to the Effective Date. Seller shall furnish Purchaser with all
   additional information concerning the Property which Purchaser may reasonably request prior
   to Closing or which relates to any Due Diligence Materials which information is in Seller’s
   possession or control or in the control of a third party agent of Seller, such as title, management
   or escrow agents. Seller shall cooperate with Purchaser and its agents in all reasonable respects
   with respect to such delivery of information and shall so direct Seller’s agents and employees.

       7.      Closing Date.

            a. Purchaser shall tender the balance of the Purchase Price and acquire the Property
               from Seller (the “Closing”) on the date (the “Closing Date”) which is the first day
               of the month following the later of (i) ten business days after Purchaser’s receipt
               of CHOW approval and (ii) ninety (90) days after the expiration of the Due
               Diligence Period.

            b. Purchaser may extend the Closing Date for up to one (1) additional thirty day
               period (the “Extension Option”). The Extension Option may be exercised by
               Purchaser upon the delivery of an additional deposit to Escrow Agent of One
               Hundred Thousand ($100,000) Dollars (“Extension Deposit”). The Extension
               Deposit shall become part of the Deposit and any disposition of the Deposit shall
               include the Extension Deposit.

       8.      Title

                 a.      Title to the Real Property conveyed hereby at Closing shall be insurable as
good and marketable at market rates with a title company authorized to do business in the State of
Rhode Island. The Property shall be conveyed to Purchaser free and clear of all liens,
encumbrances, easements, reservations, limitations, covenants, agreements, conditions and
restrictions, other than the following (the “Permitted Exceptions”): (i) real estate taxes and
assessments assessed or imposed by a public body upon the Property or the operation thereof which
are not due and payable on the Closing Date, which relate to the period from and after the Closing
Date and which have not become liens or encumbrances against the Real Property; (ii) the
requirements of any federal, state or municipal laws, ordinances, rules and regulations that do not
materially interfere with the current use of the Real Property and so long as such laws are not
violated; (iii) rights of utility companies to maintain pipes, poles, cables and wires, over, on or
under the street and/or the Real Property next to the street or running to or serving (but not under)
any building on the Real Property for the benefit of the Real Property so long as the Real Property
as currently situated does not violate any of the recorded utility easements and such rights do not
go under any paved area of the Property, under any building, or close to a building in a way which
makes it impractical to access such pipes, poles, cables and wires without incurring an additional
expense or causing damage to the building; and (iv) all Admission Agreements (as hereinafter
defined) as shown on the Census (as hereinafter defined) which is to be updated by Seller at
Closing.

              b.     Within fifteen (15) days after the Effective Date, Purchaser shall, at
Purchaser’s sole cost and expense, order a title commitment for the Property (the “Title
Commitment”) from a title company authorized to do business in the State of Rhode Island (the

                                                 5

===== PAGE 103 =====
“Title Company”) which shall be selected by Purchaser. Purchaser shall, at Purchaser’s sole cost
and expense, order a land survey of the type and character acceptable to Purchaser from a licensed
surveyor in the State of Rhode Island (the “Survey”). If the Title Commitment lists exceptions to
coverage, other than Permitted Exceptions, which are not acceptable to Purchaser, or the Survey
shows any defects to title which are not Permitted Exceptions (collectively the “Title
Objections”), Purchaser shall notify Seller of such Title Objections within ten (10) days after
receipt of the Title Commitment and the Survey (the “Purchaser’s Title Objection Notice”). In
the event Purchaser fails to deliver to Seller the Purchaser’s Title Objection Notice in the time
period set forth above, Purchaser shall be deemed to have accepted the Title Commitment and
Survey as is. Seller shall notify Purchaser of its intent to cure or not to cure the Title Objections
(the “Seller’s Title Notice”) within ten (10) days after receipt of the Purchaser’s Title Objection
Notice (the “Seller’s Title Notice Date”). In the event that Seller does not deliver to Purchaser the
Seller’s Title Notice by the Seller’s Title Notice Date, Seller shall be deemed to have elected not
to cure any Title Objections. In the event Seller elects to cure any of the Title Objections, Seller
shall cause such Title Objections to be cured within thirty (30) days of the Seller’s Title Notice or
obtain affirmative title insurance coverage over same to the satisfaction of Purchaser in its
reasonable discretion; provided, however, that Seller may request a reasonable extension of such
date and Purchaser shall grant such extension so long as any delay is based upon circumstances
beyond the reasonable control of Seller and such extension does not exceed thirty (30) additional
days, such extension does not adversely affect Purchaser’s ability to close on its financing (such
extended date the “Seller’s Title Cure Date”). Seller shall be responsible for the payment of any
recording costs associated with the cure of the Title Objections. If Seller shall not have cured all
of the Title Objections which it has elected to cure to Purchaser’s reasonable satisfaction by the
Seller’s Title Cure Date then Purchaser shall have the right to proceed to Closing without
abatement of the Purchase Price or terminate this Agreement and receive a refund of the Deposit
and Seller shall reimburse Purchaser for all of Purchaser’s out-of-pocket costs associated with the
transaction contemplated hereby which obligation shall survive termination of this Agreement,
whereupon this Agreement shall be null and void and of no further force and effect other than
those items specifically agreed upon to survive termination or earlier expiration of this Agreement.
If Seller’s Title Notice notifies Purchaser of Seller’s intent not to remove all of the Title Objections
or if Seller is deemed to have elected not to cure all of the Title Objections, Purchaser, at its option,
may on written notice (“Purchaser’s Title Reply Notice”) to Seller within ten (10) days after
receipt of Seller’s Title Notice either (i) terminate this Agreement and the Deposit shall be returned
to Purchaser and neither party shall have any further liability to the other hereunder; or (ii) accept
title pursuant hereto without any adjustment in the Purchase Price, provided, however, that Seller
shall remain obligated to remove any Title Objections which it has agreed to remove in accordance
with this Section.

                c.    Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, Seller shall remove at
Closing all monetary liens and encumbrances that affect any portion of the Property, including,
but not limited to, mortgages, liens, judgments security interests and financing statements and
Purchaser may deduct said amount thereof from the Purchase Price at Closing or, if acceptable to
the Title Company, Seller may escrow the funds necessary to remove or bond over such lien so
that the Title Company shall provide title insurance without excepting for such lien.

                d.      In the event that after the expiration of the Due Diligence Period any
additional title search or any survey shall disclose any additional Title Objections which are not
shown on the initial Title Commitment or in the Survey, the parties shall have the same rights,


                                                   6

===== PAGE 104 =====
remedies and obligations as provided for in subsections (b) and (c) of this Section 8 except that
Purchaser and Seller shall have five (5) days to send the Purchaser’s Title Objection Notice and
Seller’s Title Notice, as applicable.

        9.      Access.

                a.       Before the Due Diligence Period, during the Due Diligence Period and
thereafter through the Closing Date, Purchaser and its agents, engineers, lenders, capital sources,
partners, employees, attorneys, surveyors, appraisers, auditors and other representatives shall have
reasonable access to the Property during normal working hours accompanied by the Facility
Administrator or such other Seller designee and upon reasonable notice to Seller, subject to the
rights of residents, to inspect, examine, survey, observe, obtain engineering inspections, conduct
environmental tests and inspections and any other reasonable activity to determine the physical
and financial condition of the Property; provided such access shall at all times be consistent with
the intention to keep the potential sale contemplated hereby confidential until the expiration of the
Due Diligence Period. Seller shall not unreasonably withhold or delay consent to any such
inspection. Purchaser shall use commercially reasonable efforts in connection with all such
inspections to avoid attention of staff of the Facilities and to avoid interference with services
rendered to residents of the Facilities. Purchaser agrees to promptly repair with like materials and
in a quality workmanlike manner acceptable to Seller any damage to the Property caused by
Purchaser’s entry upon the Property and Purchaser shall indemnify Seller for any claims or
damages related to said inspections. Purchaser shall not make any invasive testing without the
consent of the Seller, which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld. In the event that invasive
testing is required by a lender as a result of any environmental survey of the Property, Seller shall
either consent to such test within three (3) days after request or Seller’s consent shall be deemed
granted. Seller may require that a representative of Seller be present at the time of such invasive
testing.

                b.      In conducting any inspections, investigations or tests of the Property,
Purchaser shall (i) not unreasonably disturb the Facility or interfere with the use of the Property;
(ii) not materially or unreasonably interfere with the operation and maintenance of the Property;
(iii) not materially damage any part of the Property or any personal property owned or held by any
tenant or any third party; (iv) not injure or otherwise cause bodily harm to Seller or its agents,
guests, invitees, contractors and employees or any tenants or their guests or invitees; (v) comply
in all material respects with all applicable laws; and (vi) not permit any liens to attach to the
Property by reason of the exercise of its rights hereunder.

         10.    Service Contracts. Purchaser shall have no obligation to assume any of the service
or maintenance contracts, vendor agreements, equipment leases, commercial leases, marketing
agreements or laundry contracts or leases (the “Service Contracts”) which relate to the Property
or the Facility and will notify the Seller of the Service Contracts that it wishes to assume at least
thirty (30) days prior to Closing (the “Service Contract Notification Date”). Seller shall provide
a termination notice to the service providers whose Service Contracts are not being assumed no
later than the earlier of (i) thirty (30) days prior to Closing or (ii) the date required for notice under
each such Service Contract, and shall provide Purchase a copy of the letter to each such provider.
In the event that the Purchaser does not notify Seller of its intent to assume any Service Contracts
by the Service Contract Notification Date, Purchaser will not assume any of the Service Contracts.
Seller will terminate any Service Contracts not assumed by Purchaser and shall be solely
responsible for same.

       11.     Employees.

                a.      All employees who work at the Property as of the Effective Date are listed
on Schedule 11(a) as such Schedule may be amended at Closing (the “Facility Employees”).
Seller shall terminate the employment of all Facility Employees effective immediately upon the
Closing. The Schedule of Facility Employees shall state the name of the employee, the position
and compensation of each employee and specify which employees are members of a union. No
later than forty five (45) days prior to Closing, Seller shall provide Purchaser with the social
security numbers and other personal information required for Purchaser to put the Rehired
Employees into its payroll system. Purchaser shall offer employment to at least 95% of the Facility
Employees to avoid the applicability of the Federal WARN Act and any State WARN Act. All
Facility Employees who are actively employed by Seller immediately prior to the Closing Date
and who have been offered and have accepted Purchaser’s offer of employment as set forth herein
(the “Rehired Employees”) shall become employed by the Purchaser as of the Closing Date.
Purchaser’s agreement herein to offer employment to Facility Employees is solely between Seller
and Purchaser and is not intended for the benefit of third parties. As such, no Facility Employee
can rely on Purchaser’s obligation to offer employment to any of the Facility Employees.
Additionally, Purchaser’s obligation as set forth herein to offer employment to the employees is
subject to any new employment terms and to any initial terms and conditions as determined by the
Purchaser. Purchaser and Seller acknowledge that Purchaser is solely responsible for all decisions
regarding Facility Employees of the Facility related to the period after Closing. Notwithstanding
the other provisions of this Agreement the parties agree, that subject to applicable law and
regulations, the Purchaser: (a) shall have no obligation to recognize any union or to assume any
of the collective bargaining agreements currently in place; (b) shall have the right to and be deemed
to have the intention not to assume any such collective bargaining agreement; and (c) shall have
the right and be deemed to have the intention to set its own initial terms and conditions of
employment for any employees of Old Operator that Purchaser may choose, in its sole discretion,
to hire.

              b.     At least seven (7) days prior to the Closing, Purchaser shall deliver a list of
employees to whom Purchaser will not offer employment (the “Excluded Employees”) to Seller
(the “Schedule of Excluded Employees”) which Schedule of Excluded Employees may be
updated by the Purchaser at any time. Seller shall notify such Excluded Employees that they will
not be hired by Purchaser and Seller shall be responsible for all obligations to such Excluded
Employees which obligation shall survive Closing.

                 c.      During the period prior to Closing, the parties agree to reasonably cooperate
and also to consult on a regular basis and coordinate their activities relating to employee matters
so as to facilitate a smooth transition of Property operations and the continued proper performance
by the Facility Employees of their respective duties up to Closing.

                d.     Purchaser shall not assume any obligation to contribute to any retirement
plan or any other obligations of Seller under the Employee Retirement Income Security Act, as
amended (“ERISA”) after the Closing Date. The Purchaser shall also not be responsible for
wages, “COBRA” medical and health care continuation coverage under Section 4980B of the
Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended (the “Code”), if any, for the benefit of the covered
employees of Seller, and their eligible dependents, regardless of whether Seller continues to
maintain group medical and health insurance coverage plans on and after the Closing Date or other
fringe benefit arrangements including, without limitation, any retirement or deferred compensation

                                                  8

plan, savings, incentive, healthcare, stock option or stock purchase plan, unemployment
compensation plan, severance pay and/or any other benefit, for any employee of Seller, or any and
all other obligations to any employee of Seller pursuant to any agreement (whether oral or written),
by law or otherwise, if any, due to or with respect to any employee of Seller or as a result of this
transaction. The Seller shall remain solely responsible for any liability arising from workers’

and any other obligations or benefits. Seller shall indemnify Purchaser for all claims not assumed
by Purchaser as well as for any other employment related obligation which indemnity shall survive

audits or inspections from any governmental agency or is aware of any reason why it is impractical

       14.     Seller’s Obligation Pending Closing. From the Effective Date through the
Closing Date, unless this Agreement is earlier terminated, Seller covenants to perform the
following obligations:

                a.      Seller shall continue to operate and maintain the Property and the Facility
as a skilled nursing facility, in accordance with its respective licenses and Permits and consistent
with its operation and maintenance at the time of execution of this Agreement, so that at Closing,
the Facility and all of the Property, including the personal property, fixtures, equipment and
systems necessary to operate the Facility, including but not limited to the HVAC, electrical and
plumbing systems are and on the Closing Date will be in good working condition and repair and
in working order as of the date hereof subject to ordinary wear and tear. In this respect, Seller
shall (1) comply with all material regulations, lawful orders and directives of all Governmental
Authorities with respect to the Facility and the Property; (2) continue to comply and perform in
accordance with the material terms of all of the agreements which relate to the Property and the
Facility, the Service Contracts, any provider agreements and the Admission Agreements; (3)
maintain, repair and replace the Property so that the Property is in good working order and in
substantially the same condition as at the Effective Date, subject to ordinary wear and tear; (4)
continue to advertise and offer the Facility’s space for residency in a manner consistent with past
practices; (5) continue to manage the Facility by billing for and collecting charges and otherwise
enforcing the Admission Agreements, as the case may be, in a manner consistent with past
practices; (6) take all commercially reasonable actions consistent with past practices intended to
preserve the present residency occupancy levels and payor mix at the Facility; (7) retain the
goodwill with all of the suppliers, clientele, residents and others having business relations with
Seller and the Facility in a manner consistent with past practices; (8) continue to hire employees
to the extent necessary to operate the Facility in accordance with past practices, provided, that Old
Operator shall not hire any new property manager, administrator, director of nursing and the
directors of sales and marketing without the consent of the Purchaser which consent may not be
unreasonable conditioned, withheld or delayed; (9) not decrease any rates of private pay residents
of the Facility without the prior written consent of Purchaser; (10) maintain existing insurance
coverage until Closing, and advise Purchaser of any material change in coverage; (11) not remove
any of the Assets from the Facility unless the same is replaced by Assets of a substantially like
kind and of equal or higher value, except for Excluded Assets; (12) not admit any residents that do
not have a confirmed payor source or application pending for public or private insurance and Seller
reasonably determines that such application is likely to be approved (13) not take any action which
would materially reduce the Purchaser’s reimbursement rate after Closing and (14) not agree to
any bonuses which would be paid following the Closing.

               b.      Seller will not, without Purchaser’s prior written consent, modify or amend
any of the agreements which relate to the Property or the Facility or enter into or renew any new
Service Contracts or other agreements that are not cancelable at Closing. Seller shall not create or
allow to be created any liens, encumbrances, covenants, restrictions or easements affecting or
encumbering the Property which are not shown on the Title Commitment or otherwise disclosed
in this Agreement. Seller will not sell, assign or convey any right, title, or interest whatever in the

                                                  17

===== PAGE 115 =====
Property to any third party other than Admission Agreements in the form previously submitted to
the Purchaser which are entered into in the normal course of business.

                 c.      Seller shall cure all violations and other deficiencies identified by any
Governmental Authority (all such matters collectively the “Violations”) relating to the Facility
and the Property, pay all penalties associated therewith and prepare and implement any Plan of
Correction required by any Governmental Authority. In the event at Closing there are uncorrected
Violations relating to the Facility for which Seller has had insufficient time to cure, or any
Governmental Authority survey or inspection conducted prior to the Closing Date identifies any
Violations at the Facility which Seller has had insufficient time to cure, Purchaser shall proceed to
Closing so long as all Violations are from DOH and relate to the provision of care to the residents
at the Facility and none of the Violations are of a material nature. Violations of a material nature
includes (1) any Violation which is a level G or higher, (2) any notice of intent to revoke or limit
any Facility license or Medicare/Medicaid certification, or (3) any restriction on reimbursements
or new admission (each of the above a “High Level Deficiency”). Purchaser shall not be required
to proceed to Closing in the event that at any time prior to Closing there are one or more High
Level Deficiencies outstanding at the Facility or if there are Violations affecting the Property or
the Facility that are not from the Department of Health and in such event, Purchaser shall have the
option to either (i) adjourn the Closing Date for a specified time to allow the Seller time to correct
such Violations, provided, that if the Violations are not fully cured and cleared to the satisfaction
of the relevant Government Authority within such specified time, Purchaser shall have the right to
terminate this Agreement and shall be entitled to a return of the Deposit or can further postpone
the Closing for another specified time in accordance with the previous clause, and provided further,
that no such postponement shall occur if it shall cause DOH not to issue a new license to Purchaser
effective as of the Closing Date; or (ii) terminate this Agreement and in such event the Purchaser
shall be entitled to a return of the Deposit. In the event that at any time the Facility receives 2 or
more High Level Deficiencies, the Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this Agreement and
shall be entitled to a return of the Deposit.

               d.      Seller shall also be responsible to correct any condition for which the
Facility has previously received a waiver but such waiver cannot be transferred to the Purchaser

condition upon its notice thereof, provided however, if Seller believes a waiver is obtainable for
such condition, Purchaser and Seller shall cooperate in obtaining such waiver and shall use
commercially reasonable efforts to obtain such waiver prior to Closing.

Department of Health or does not relate to the provision of care to the residents at the Facility,
Purchaser shall have the right to either (i) terminate this Agreement and shall be entitled to a return
of the Deposit or (ii) adjourn the Closing Date to allow Seller a reasonable time to cure such
Violations, provided, that if the Violations are not cured to satisfaction of the relevant government
agency within a reasonable time, Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this Agreement and
shall be entitled to a return of the Deposit.

              g.     Purchaser’s receipt of an owner’s title insurance policy, or “marked-up”
title commitment with policy to follow, at filed rates in accordance with Section 8 above.

                 h.     None of the following shall have occurred prior to the Closing: (i) receipt
of notice of potential revocation, suspension, restriction or refusal to renew any license or permit
or there are any restrictions on admissions or reimbursement, (ii) the Facility is designated as a
special focus facility or similar designation or is placed on any watchlist, (iii) the Facility is subject
to open investigations or qui tam litigation, (iv) the Facility is not in substantial compliance with
Medicaid and Medicare regulations, (v) the Nursing Home Facility receives any violation Level G
or higher through Closing, (vi) EBITDAR of the Facility for the month of Closing (or for the
previous full month if Closing occurs prior to the twenty first (21st) of the month), or for any full
month prior to Closing, is not ninety five (95%) percent of the average monthly EBITDAR for the
six (6) months prior to the Effective Date which for clarity is $______________(vii) general and
Medicare census as of the Closing Date, or for any full month prior to the Closing Date, is not 95%
of the average monthly general and Medicare census for the six months prior to the Effective
Date which for clarity is ______ general and _______Medicare, and (viii) no event has occurred
which may reasonably result in any of the foregoing. In addition to Purchaser’s other remedies
for failure of the conditions set forth herein, in the event that at Closing, the EBITDAR or census
is less than ninety-five (95%) percent as set forth herein (not including residents who have no
confirmed payor source), Purchaser shall be entitled to a credit to the Purchase Price in an amount
equal to $7,500 for each bed which is below the census threshold and a credit equal to the
EBITDAR deficiency divided by twelve (12%) percent to compensate the Purchaser for the
reduced EBITDAR.

              i.     The absence of any previously undisclosed material claim or liability to
which the Purchaser or the Facility will be subject if Purchaser acquires the Property other than
Permitted Exceptions and as otherwise provided for herein.

        20.     Costs. Seller shall be responsible for the payment of: (i) any prepayment and
termination fees or penalties with respect to any financing, monetary encumbrances or liens
affecting the Property; (ii) all of the standard real estate transfer tax incurred in connection with

recording all of the closing documents, any mortgage tax and for the cost of the title policy. The
parties shall split the cost of any escrow or closing fee. The title company doing the Closing shall
be the escrow agent (the “Escrow Agent”) Each party shall be responsible for its own attorneys
and professional fees. All other items not set forth in this Agreement shall be allocated in
accordance with local custom.

       21.     Closing Prorations.

                a.      At Closing, Seller shall transfer or credit to Purchaser, all of Seller’s right,
title and interest in and to all deposits required to be held under the Admission Agreements,
resident trust accounts, patient trust funds security, pet deposits, and other deposits and any private
pay payments for services to be rendered subsequent to the Closing Date, if any (plus interest
thereon to the extent earned or required to be paid, if any) and no additional payment or adjustment
shall be made therefor.

                b.      Real estate taxes shall be prorated in accordance with local custom. To the
extent that Seller is responsible for taxes which are to be paid following Closing and such taxes
are not determined as of the Closing Date, Seller shall put into escrow with the Escrow Agent an
amount sufficient to pay the taxes which are Seller’s responsibility plus five (5%) percent, as
determined by the Title Company. If any additional or supplemental taxes are assessed against
the Property for back assessments, corrections to previous tax bills or other events occurring before
the Closing Date, including without limitation the imposition of any retroactive assessments
relating to improvements made prior to the Closing Date, the parties shall make any necessary
adjustments after the Closing. This Section is to survive Closing for one (1) year.

               c.      At the Closing, Purchaser and Seller shall adjust for rents and other charges
collected, assessments, water and sewer service as of the Closing Date, Service Contracts to the
extent assumed by the Purchaser and other customary prorations. The parties shall prorate any
signing or other bonus received by the Seller for any of the Service Contracts or other agreements
being assumed by the Purchaser. Seller shall obtain final meter readings immediately prior to
Closing. In the event final meter readings are not available, readings shall be estimated based on
the most recent reading and the parties shall agree to reconcile any discrepancies in the actual
amount upon receipt of final readings, which obligation shall survive closing and delivery of the
deed. Seller shall provide the Title Company with any escrows required for any lienable utilities.

                d.      All installment payments of special assessments, if any, which are due and
payable at any time prior to the Closing Date shall be paid by Seller on or prior to the Closing
Date. All installment payments of special assessments, if any, which are due and payable at any
time on or after the Closing Date shall be paid by Purchaser. Notwithstanding the above, in the
event any special assessments are due and payable after the Closing Date and such assessments
are specifically identified as applying to matters occurring prior to the Closing Date, the Seller
shall be responsible for such payment but only to the extent that such assessments relate to periods
of time prior to the Closing Date. In addition, in the event any special assessments are due and
payable on or prior to the Closing Date and such assessments are specifically identified as applying
to matters occurring after the Closing Date, the Purchaser shall be responsible for such payment.

                                                  25

===== PAGE 123 =====
Notwithstanding the above, any payments of special assessments for improvements which are
installed at the Property as of the expiration of the Due Diligence Period shall be the responsibility
of the Seller and Purchaser shall receive a credit therefor.

                e.      At Purchaser’s election, at Closing Seller shall either (i) pay out to all
employees all accrued Employee Benefits for all Employees so that New Operator shall have no
obligations to any of the Rehired Employees for any Employee Benefits and in such event,
notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, New Operator shall have no liability to
any Rehired Employees for any matters related to prior to Closing, or (ii) provide a credit to
Purchaser in the amount of all accrued Employee Benefits for all Rehired Employees and such
credit shall also include all associated taxes and other amounts which Purchaser will be required
to pay in connection with the accrued Employee Benefits which include, but are not limited to,
FICA, federal unemployment taxes, social security, state payroll and other taxes related to the
foregoing (the “Payroll Taxes”). Seller will pay out to the Facility Employees all accrued paid
time off which is required to be paid out in accordance with applicable law. Seller will provide
Purchaser a credit for any bonuses which may be paid out following the Closing.

                f.     Purchaser shall receive a credit for any provider, bed or other tax due to any
government agency for the period prior to the Closing Date, including without limitation, Seller’s
pro rata portion of such provider or bed tax for the period during which the Closing occurs.

                g.     Prepaid and unearned deposits and payments by patients, all amounts which
are unearned and returnable to residents, all entrance fees and other such third party prepaid items
relating to periods after the Effective Time or which are not earned by the Facility, shall be
transferred to Purchaser, or the amounts thereof credited to Purchaser, at the Closing.

               h.      Intentionally Deleted.

        i.      The Closing shall become effective and Purchaser shall take over the operations of
the Facility as of 12:01 AM on the Closing Date (the “Effective Time”) and, except as otherwise
set forth in this Agreement, Seller shall be entitled to all benefits and be responsible for all
obligations of the Facility related to the period prior to the Effective Time and the Purchaser shall
be entitled to the benefits and be responsible for the liabilities related to the period from and after
the Effective Time. All prorations shall be made as of the Closing Date based upon the most
current information then available. The parties agree that except as provided for herein, to the
extent final prorations for anything other than real estate taxes cannot be determined because of
lack of data, information or readings accurate as of the Closing Date, the parties shall adjust any
prorations made on the Closing Statement based on the best information available and shall re-
prorate such amounts when the information becomes available but no later than sixty (60) days
after the Closing. Both parties shall respond promptly to requests for data, information or readings
from the other and execute any additional addenda to the Closing Statement as may be reasonably
necessary to reflect any adjustments. This Paragraph shall survive Closing.

       22.     Accounts Receivable

                a.       All payments related to services provided from and after the Closing Time
shall belong to the Purchaser and all payments related to services provided prior to the Closing
Time shall belong to the Seller. To the extent that either party receives payments which belong to
the other party, it shall deliver said payment to the other party within five (5) days of receipt and

                                                  26

===== PAGE 124 =====
at least one time each month the parties shall deliver to each other a schedule of amounts received
together with the remittances for reconciliation.

               b.      Until the end of the twelve (12) months from the Closing Date (the
“Receivables Date”), all funds received which are not from Medicaid, Medicare or other third
party payor (the “Private Pay Receivables”) shall belong to the Seller or Purchaser as appropriate
in each instance in which the payment is identifiable to services provided by Seller or Purchaser.
Subsequent to the Receivables Date, all Private Pay Receivables received by either party shall
belong to the New Operator. Payments of Private Pay Receivables which are not identifiable to a
specific period which are received within ninety (90) days after the Closing Date shall belong to
the Seller and all such Private Pay Receivables received more than ninety (90) days after the
Closing Date shall belong to the Purchaser until all Private Pay Receivables owed to Purchaser are
current and then to Seller in the amount of any unpaid installments of Private Pay Receivables
owed to Seller as stated in the Schedule of Seller Accounts Receivable delivered to Purchaser at
Closing. Purchaser shall cooperate with Seller in regards to pending approvals for reimbursement
of services for residents at the Facility as of the Closing Date. Any supplemental payments,
earmarks or other payments made under any programs which are not a direct payment for services
provided prior to the Closing Date, which payments are made following the Closing shall belong
to the New Operator. Any funds received by the Facility following the date which is three (3)
years following the Closing Date regardless of the period for which such amounts relate, shall
belong to Purchaser.

               c.       In order to facilitate an efficient determination and distribution of the pre
and post-closing funds to be received by Seller following the Closing, Seller and Purchaser agree
to provide each other with its bank account information and the parties shall agree on the
appropriate allocation of such funds within ten (10) days of receipt. The Purchaser shall cover the
costs of reconciliation.

               d.      This Section 22 shall survive Closing.

       23.     Post Closing Obligations.

                a.      Seller shall timely prepare and file or cause to be prepared and filed all
Medicare, Medicaid and third-party payor cost reports for periods prior to the Closing Date and
shall deliver copies of such reports to Purchaser, not later than ten (10) days following their timely
filing with the appropriate governmental agency or payor.

                b.     For periods prior to the Closing, Seller agrees to fully cooperate with
Purchaser in connection with any desk audit or full audit by any applicable governmental authority
or regulatory agency, including without limitation, providing Purchaser with any available
necessary records, documentation, supporting schedules and personnel in order to properly support
the dollar figures, RUG rates and patient classifications/characterizations contained in Seller’s cost
reports so that Purchaser’s Medicaid or Medicare reimbursement can be maximized in accordance
with applicable law.

                c.      Seller agrees that for a period of one (1) year after the Closing Date, Seller,
or any of its related or affiliated entities shall not (i) solicit or hire any of the employees who are
employed at any time after the date which is thirty (30) days before the Effective Date until twelve
(12) months after such employee is no longer working for Purchaser, or (ii) solicit, recruit or admit


                                                  27

===== PAGE 125 =====
any residents of the Facility who are residents as of the Closing Date in any other facility owned
or operated by the Seller or any affiliate. Seller acknowledges that Purchaser’s damages for
Seller’s breach of this provision are difficult to ascertain and in the event Seller hires any such
employee or admits any resident without Purchaser’s written consent, Purchaser shall be entitled
to liquidated damages in the amount of Fifty Thousand Dollars ($50,000) for each employee so
hired or resident so admitted. In addition, Seller acknowledges that damages may not be sufficient
to reimburse Purchaser for any breach of this provision and Seller hereby consents to injunctive
relief precluding Seller from violating the provisions of this Section.

                d.      Seller shall be responsible for all monetary penalties assessed for violations
which directly relate to Seller’s acts or omissions prior to Closing at the Facility regardless of
whether or not such violation was actually determined prior to Closing. In the event that prior or
subsequent to Closing the Facility is assessed or will be assessed a penalty for a deficiency that
occurred prior to Closing or for a deficiency or which existed as of the Closing which directly
relates to Seller’s acts or omission prior to Closing, whether or not such penalty was actually
assessed prior to Closing. Seller shall be responsible for all monetary penalties assessed for
violations that occur prior to Closing, provided, however, that to the extent that the penalty is
assessed on a per diem basis, Seller will only be responsible for such per diem penalty through the
Closing Date.

                 e.      In the event that within thirty (30) days following the Closing Date, any
special inspection is conducted in response to a complaint, not initiated by the Purchaser or any
Affiliate, relating to the Facility or other Property (other than the Real Property), directly arising
out of acts or omissions of the Seller prior to the Closing Date, and such inspection identifies any
material violation or deficiency which is due to such action or omission prior to the Closing Date,
Seller shall be fully responsible for the reasonable cost of such correction of any such violation or
deficiency and in the event that Seller does not pay for or reimburse the Purchaser for the costs to
correct such violations within three (3) days of demand therefor, Purchaser shall have the right to
have funds released from any escrow created under this Agreement but such release shall not
release Seller from its responsibility to reimburse Purchaser for such amounts. Seller shall
maintain tail insurance required under this Agreement.

                f.       Seller hereby agrees that Purchaser shall have the right to bill Medicare and
Medicaid under Seller’s respective Medicare and Medicaid provider agreements and provider
numbers until such time as Purchaser shall begin to receive payments in its own name or, as it
relates to Medicaid, until such time as New Operator receives License Approval. Seller also agrees
for Purchaser to bill all third party payors under its existing agreements until such time as Purchaser
shall have obtained a new provider agreement with such third party providers. Seller shall deliver
all funds received for services provided after Closing to Purchaser within five (5) business days
after receipt of said funds. Seller hereby agrees for Purchaser to file a UCC financing statement
to secure its interest in said funds and may assign such lien to its lender and Old Operator agrees
to execute any such document which evidences its consent for the filing of financing statements
against the post closing receivables to be received by Old Operator.

                g.      Seller shall deliver to Purchaser all records related to residents who were at
the Facility within the twelve (12) months prior to Closing as well as electronic delivery of all
records in electronic form that Purchaser requests at Closing. In addition, all records which are at
the Facility may remain at the Facility at no cost. Seller hereby indemnifies Purchaser for any
claims related to the records. Furthermore, Seller hereby authorizes Purchaser to respond to any

                                                  28

===== PAGE 126 =====
request for paper records which are stored at the Facility from any person or governmental
authority, although Purchaser shall have no obligation to do so, and Seller shall maintain its
responsibility to respond to any audits or other request for records. Purchaser is hereby authorized
to destroy any records which are older than seven years without further consent from the Seller.

                h.      Subsequent to the Closing Date, Seller shall be entitled to obtain at Seller’s
sole cost from the Purchaser the originals or copies of any Records delivered to the Purchaser, for

       24.     Indemnity and Escrow.

               a.      In the event that on or after the Closing Date any governmental authority,
agency, fiscal intermediary, medical administrative contractor or third-party payor withholds,
recoups, recaptures or recovers any alleged overpayment to Seller or Old Operator or any alleged
underpayment by Seller or Old Operator of any tax, license fee and/or assessment, or if any
governmental authority, agency, fiscal intermediary, medical administrative contractor or third-
party payor making payments to Purchaser for services performed at the Facility on or after the
Closing Date makes a recoupment of payment for any services performed before the Closing Date
or if any governmental authority withholds any payment from Purchaser for any reason related to
Seller or Old Operator (all such events shall be referred to as “Recoupment”) the Purchaser shall
provide Seller written notice of such Recoupment within ten (10) days following the date on which
Purchaser receives notice of the Recoupment (the “Recoupment Notice”) but the failure of
Purchaser to provide timely notice to Seller shall not affect Seller’s indemnity so long as Seller
has not been prejudiced in its appeal of such Recoupment. Following delivery by Purchaser of the
Recoupment Notice, Purchaser shall have the right but not the obligation to assert a claim against
the Indemnity Escrow Agreement for any such Recoupment. If a Recoupment is being effected in
installments, Purchaser’s right to assert claims against the Indemnity Escrow Agreement shall
follow the same installment amounts. Nothing herein shall be deemed to prevent or restrict Seller
from contesting, appealing and/or settling any such Recoupment but such contest shall not delay
or affect in any way, Seller’s obligation to reimburse Purchaser. Purchaser agrees to fully
cooperate with any such contest, appeal or settlement.

                b.     Except as otherwise provided, only if the Closing has occurred and then
from and after the Closing, Seller jointly and severally hereby indemnifies and agrees to defend
and hold Purchaser and its successors, assigns, affiliates, managers, members, agents, servants,
employees, and any tenant operator of the Facility, harmless from and against any and all claims,
demands, obligations, losses, liabilities, damages, recoveries and deficiencies (including interest,
penalties and reasonable attorneys’ fees, costs and expenses) (collectively the “Losses”) which
any of them may suffer as a result of any matter which relates to the period prior to Closing or to
Seller’s obligations prior to and after Closing, including, but not limited to, the following events

                                                  29

===== PAGE 127 =====
(“Indemnification Events”): (i) the untruth of any of the representations of Seller or the breach
of any of the warranties of Seller herein or given pursuant hereto; (ii) any default or other failure
by Seller, whether prior to or after Closing, in the performance of any of its commitments,
covenants or obligations under this Agreement; (iii) any litigation, arbitration proceedings,
administrative actions, complaints or investigations which relate to the Facility or the Property
prior to the Closing Date; (iv) any liability not assumed by Purchaser including, but not limited to,
liabilities arising under the Admission Agreements, the provider agreements, the third-party payor
agreements and the Service Contracts, for the period prior to the Closing Date; (v) taxes, penalties
charges or fees related to acts or events for the period prior to the Closing Date or related to this
transaction, including but not limited to any civil monetary penalties and bed tax, regardless of
when assessed; (vi) any employment related obligations of the Seller and the Facility not assumed
by Purchaser, including, but not limited to, any obligations of Seller and Old Operator under
ERISA or any collective bargaining agreement, any discrepancies in the Schedule of Employee
Benefits, any claim for wages, or other amounts not credited to Purchaser or any claim resulting
from this transaction; (vii) the failure to correct or abate any violations, deficiencies or other
obligations related to the Property and the Facility; (viii) Recoupments; (ix) any other matter
related to the period prior to the Effective Time; (x) all damages incurred by the New Operator as
a result of Old Operator’s failure to timely file after Closing any Medicare, Medicaid and third
party payor cost reports for the period prior to Closing or to deliver any records requested by
Purchaser; and (xi) any claim made by any governmental authority related to Seller or its affiliates
and the members, and principals thereof; (xii) any bonuses claimed by employees pursuant to any
alleged agreement with the Seller; and (xiii) any loss incurred by the Purchaser related to residents
with no payor source.

                 c.      Only if the Closing has occurred and then from and after the Closing,
Purchaser shall defend and indemnify Seller, and its successors, assigns, affiliates, managers,
partners, agents, servants, employees (the “Seller Indemnitees”), harmless from and against any
and all claims, demands, obligations, losses, liabilities, damages, recoveries and deficiencies
(including interest, penalties and reasonable attorneys’ fees, costs and expenses) which any of
them may suffer as a result of any of the following events that Seller Indemnitees incur as a result
of, or with respect to: (i) the untruth of any of the representations of Purchaser or the breach of any
of the warranties of Purchaser herein or given pursuant hereto; (ii) any default by Purchaser in the
performance of any of its commitments, covenants or obligations under this Agreement, (iii) with
respect to any litigation, arbitration proceedings, administrative actions, complaints or
investigations which relate to the ownership and use of the Property by Purchaser following the
Closing Date; (iv) penalties charges or fees related to acts or events for the period after the Closing
Date, including but not limited to any civil monetary penalties and bed tax, except as otherwise set
forth in this Agreement; (vi) any other matter related to the period after the Effective Time except
as set forth in this Agreement; or (viii) any claim made by any governmental authority related to
Purchaser or its affiliates and the members, and principals thereof which relates to the period from
and after the Closing Time.

                d.     If any party (the “Indemnitee”) receives notice of any claim or the
commencement of any proceeding with respect to which any other party (or parties) is obligated
to provide indemnification (the “Indemnifying Party”) pursuant to this Section 24, the Indemnitee
shall give the Indemnifying Party written notice thereof, so that the Indemnifying Party shall not
been prejudiced in its defense of such indemnification event. In any event, the Indemnitee, the
Indemnifying Party and the Indemnifying Party’s counsel shall cooperate in the compromise of,


                                                  30

===== PAGE 128 =====
settlement or defense against, any such asserted liability. Both the Indemnitee and the
Indemnifying Party may participate in the defense of such asserted liability and neither may settle
or compromise any claim over the reasonable objection of the other. Notwithstanding anything to
the contrary contained herein, either party may, at its option, assume control of the defense or
resolution of any such matter on its own behalf if such party reasonably believes that the defense
or resolution of such matter might materially and adversely affect the interests of such party and
so long as the other party has not commenced a compromise of, settlement or defense against an
asserted liability within thirty (30) days after the date of the notice of any claim, provided that such
party shall continue to be obligated to indemnify the other party in connection with such matter
and that the other party may not settle or compromise any such matter without the consent of such
party which shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed. If the Indemnifying Party
chooses to defend any claim, the Indemnitee shall make available to the Indemnifying Party, at
reasonable times and upon reasonable notice, any books, records or other documents within its
control that are necessary or appropriate for such defense. Purchaser’s knowledge of any default
or other matter prior to Closing or any update to any Schedule provided prior to Closing, shall not
affect Seller’s indemnification obligations set forth herein.

                 e.      In order to secure the indemnities provided by Seller and other obligations
of Seller provided for herein, at Closing, Seller and Purchaser agree to enter into an Indemnity
Escrow Agreement in substantially the form of Exhibit D (the “Indemnity Escrow Agreement”)
under which an amount equal to Five Hundred Thousand Dollars ($500,000) shall be held in
escrow (the “Indemnity Escrow Fund”) to be used to cover Seller’s indemnity and other
obligations hereunder. The Indemnity Escrow Agreement shall provide that the Indemnity Escrow
Fund shall be held by the Escrow Agent in an interest-bearing account and all earnings shall accrue
to the escrow account and be disbursed with the escrow funds. The Indemnity Escrow Agreement
shall set out a procedure for claims to be made by Purchaser and for all payments from the
Indemnity Escrow Fund to be paid based upon joint escrow instructions signed by Seller and
Purchaser hereunder. Subject to the Seller’s compliance with the provisions of Section 22(c), on
the date which is eighteen (18) months from the Closing Date, Seller shall be entitled to receive
half of the original amount of the Indemnity Escrow Fund less the amount of any prior payment
and less the amount of any pending claims. Upon the third anniversary of the Closing Date, Seller
shall be entitled to receive the remaining balance of the Indemnity Escrow Fund, less the amount
of any prior payment and less the amount of any pending claims. Purchaser covenants and agrees
that it shall first seek recovery against the Indemnity Escrow but that nothing contained in this
Section shall be deemed to limit Seller’s indemnification obligation to the amount of the
Indemnification Escrow. Purchaser covenants and agrees that it shall first seek recovery against
the Indemnity Escrow Agreement but that nothing contained in this Section shall be deemed to
limit Seller’s indemnification obligation to the amount of the Indemnification Escrow Agreement
for claims made by third parties for matters related to the period prior to Closing.

              f.      The obligations of Seller and Purchaser pursuant to this Section shall
survive Closing until such time as neither party have any obligations to each other, provided,
however, that in any event, to the extent that there are Indemnification Events for which an
indemnity has been claimed, then this indemnity shall survive until the resolution of such
Indemnification Events.

       25.     Casualty Loss.



                                                  31

===== PAGE 129 =====
                 a.     The risk of loss or damage to the Property caused by fire or other casualty
prior to the Closing shall be borne by Seller. If prior to Closing, the Property, or any part thereof,
is damaged as the result of fire or other casualty such that the cost of repair or replacement, is
reasonably likely to exceed Fifty Thousand Dollars ($50,000), or if a material part of the Property
is taken or is designated to be taken through a condemnation action or is conveyed in connection
with any eminent domain action, Seller shall notify Purchaser of such damage, destruction or
taking, (“Seller’s Casualty Notice”) within five (5) days after such casualty, which notice shall
specify all relevant insurance information, and Purchaser shall, within five (5) days after receiving
Seller’s Casualty Notice, notify Seller (“Purchaser’s Casualty Notice”) of Purchaser’s election
to either (i) terminate this Agreement, in which event the Deposit shall be returned to Purchaser
and neither party shall have any further liability to the other except for repair and indemnity
obligations pursuant to Section 9, or (ii) accept title to the Property without any abatement of the
Purchase Price whatsoever, in which event at the Closing all of the insurance or condemnation
proceeds or right to such proceeds shall be assigned by Seller to Purchaser and Seller shall credit
Purchaser for the amount of any deductible. In the event that Purchaser elects to proceed to
Closing, Seller shall not settle any claim or use any of the funds without the consent of the
Purchaser. In the event that Purchaser fails to timely send the Purchaser’s Casualty Notice,
Purchaser shall be deemed to have elected to terminate this Agreement as set forth in option (i)
herein.

                 b.      If prior to Closing, the Property, or any part thereof, is damaged but the cost
of repair is not reasonably likely to exceed Fifty Thousand Dollars ($50,000), or if an immaterial
part of the Property is taken or is designated to be taken through a condemnation action or is
conveyed in connection with any eminent domain action, Seller shall send Purchaser the Seller’s
Casualty Notice within five (5) days after such casualty or condemnation notice stating its intent
to either (i) repair the damage to the Property, or (ii) if the casualty is fully insured assign all of
the insurance or condemnation proceeds or right to such proceeds to Purchaser and Seller shall
credit Purchaser for the amount of any deductible and in such event, Seller shall not settle any
claim or use any of the funds without the consent of the Purchaser. If Seller elects to repair the
Property per item (i) above, in the event that Seller does not complete the repairs within sixty (60)
days from the date of Seller’s Casualty Notice, Purchaser shall have the right to either terminate
this Agreement or to proceed to Closing with a credit to the Purchase Price in an amount necessary
to restore the Property to the same condition as of the Effective Date but not less than the amount
provided by the insurance or condemnation award to repair such damage. If Seller elects not to
repair, and the insurance or condemnation proceeds are insufficient to repair the Property to its
prior condition, the Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this Agreement, the Deposit shall
be returned to Purchaser and neither party shall have any further liability to the other except for
repair and indemnity obligations pursuant to Section 9.

               c.      For purposes of this Section, a material part of the Property is any part of
the Property for which the cost to repair or replace would be more than $50,000 or, if any access
to the Property is removed or if such a taking materially affects the owner’s ability to operate the
Property in the same manner as currently being operated.

                a.      If Purchaser defaults under this Agreement, which shall include a material

liquidated damages (the said amount having been agreed to as liquidated damages due to the
difficulty and inconvenience of measuring and ascertaining actual damages) and the parties hereto
shall thereby be relieved of all obligations hereunder other than those specifically stated to survive
termination of this Agreement. Seller hereby waives all other rights and remedies in law or equity.

                 b.    If Seller defaults under this Agreement which shall include a material
misrepresentation by the Seller or Seller’s failure to materially comply with any of its obligations
set forth in this Agreement, the Purchaser may terminate this Agreement and shall receive a return
of the Deposit; in addition, Purchaser shall be entitled to reimbursement of its out of pocket costs

             a. Purchaser shall not be deemed in default of this Agreement for any delay or failure
to fulfill any obligation, including but not limited to Closing, hereunder or thereunder so long as

beyond the Purchaser’s control, war, acts of hostilities, acts of terrorism, declaration of a state of
emergency, pandemic, computer virus effecting any governmental authority which is required for
the Closing, natural disaster, any material disruption in the financial, banking or securities markets,
or any condition which causes lenders to pull lending commitments, provided such claiming party
provides the non-claiming party notice of such even causing the Force Majeure within five (5)
days of such event occurring.

             b. In the event of any such excused delay, the time for performance of such
obligations shall be extended for a period equal to the time lost by reason of the delay plus fifteen
(15) days. In the event that the delay, frustration, hindrance, or delay caused by the Force Majeure
set forth herein lasts for a period of more than sixty (60) days, or is reasonably likely to last for
more than sixty (60) days, Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this Agreement and in such
event the Deposit shall be returned to Purchaser.

Quality Assurance and Performance Improvement (QAPI) Program
– Feedback, Data and Monitoring
                                             Policy Statement
The QAPI program is based on the collection information obtained from data, self-assessment and systems of
feedback. Information is collected, evaluated and monitored by the QAPI committee.

                                Policy Interpretation and Implementation
   1. Information obtained about the quality of care and services delivered to residents is evaluated and
      monitored by the QAPI committee in order to identify problems that are high risk, high volume or problem
      prone and to guide decisions regarding opportunities for improvement.
   2. The QAPI process focuses on identifying systems and processes that may be problematic and could be
      contributing to avoidable negative outcomes related to resident care, quality of life, resident safety,
      resident choice or resident autonomy, and on making a good faith effort to correct or mitigate these
      outcomes.
   3. Systems and tools used to identify, collect and evaluate data from all departments to monitor
      performance indicators include, but are not limited to the following:
      a. Self-assessment tools:
           Ongoing facility assessment
           Monthly resident census data
           Annual Survey results
           QAPI self-assessment tools
      b. Data collection tools:
           Monthly surveillance reports for quality of care indicators
           Monthly quality measures reports
           Monthly staff turnover reports
           Monthly summary of accident and incident reports (and/or other adverse event tracking tools)
      c. Feedback from staff, residents and families:
           Resident and family satisfaction surveys
           Staff suggestions and exit interviews
           Feedback from resident council meetings
           Complaint survey results
   4. Data and information collected are reviewed by the committee and prioritized according to the risk,
      volume, and potential problems.
   5. Root cause analysis is conducted to identify problematic processes and systems that need to be
      addressed.
   6. Corrective actions and performance improvement activities are initiated and monitored. The committee
      tracks and documents the progress of existing initiatives as well as newly identified ones, as part of the
      ongoing QAPI process.




                                                                                               continues on next page

Quality Assurance and Performance Improvement (QAPI) Program
– Governance and Leadership
                                             Policy Statement
The quality assurance and performance improvement program is overseen and implemented by the QAPI
committee, which reports its findings, actions and results to the administrator and governing body.

                               Policy Interpretation and Implementation
   1. The administrator, whether a member of the QAPI committee or not, is ultimately responsible for the
      QAPI program, and for interpreting its results and findings to the governing body.
   2. The governing body is responsible for ensuring that the QAPI program:
      a. is implemented and maintained to address identified priorities;
      b. is sustained through transitions of leadership and staffing;
      c. is adequately resourced and funded, including the provision of money, time, equipment, training and
          staff coverage sufficient to conduct the activities of the program;
      d. is based on data, resident and staff input, and other information that measures performance; and
      e. focuses on problems and opportunities that reflect processes, functions and services provided to the
          residents.
   3. The QAPI coordinator coordinates the activities of the QAPI committee.
   4. The responsibilities of the QAPI committee are to:
      a. collect and analyze performance indicator data and other information;
      b. identify, evaluate, monitor and improve facility systems and processes that support the delivery of
          care and services;
      c. identify and help to resolve negative outcomes and/or care quality problems identified during the
          QAPI process;
      d. utilize root cause analysis to help identify where identified problems point to underlying systematic
          problems;
      e. help departments, consultants and ancillary services implement systems to correct potential and
          actual issues in quality of care;
      f. establish benchmarks and goals by which to measure performance improvement;
      g. coordinate the development, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation of performance
          improvement projects to achieve specific goals; and
      h. communicate all phases of the QAPI process to the administrator and governing body through sharing
          meeting minutes, committee activities and results of QAPI activities.
   5. The committee has the full authority to oversee the implementation of the QAPI program, including, but
      not limited to, the following:
      a. Establishing performance and outcome indicators for quality of care and services delivered in the
          facility;
      b. Choosing and implementing tools that best capture and measure data about the chosen indicators;




                                                                                             continues on next page

===== PAGE 151 =====
Quality Assurance Performance Improvement – Rhode
Island
     A. The facility develops and implements a quality improvement program and establishes a
        quality improvement committee. The governing body ensures that this program is
        effective, ongoing, nursing facility-wide and has a written plan of implementation.
     B. The facility designates a qualified individual, determined by the nursing facility’s
        administrator, to coordinate and manage the quality improvement program.
            a. The nursing facility’s quality improvement committee includes at least the
                following members:
                      i. The nursing facility administrator;
                     ii. The director of nursing;
                    iii. The medical director;
                    iv. A social worker; and
                     v. A representative of dietary services.
     C. The quality improvement committee meets at least quarterly; maintains records of all
        quality improvement activities; and keeps records of committee meetings.
     D. The quality improvement committee for the facility annually reviews and approves the
        quality improvement plan. Upon request, this plan shall be available to the public.
     E. The facility written quality improvement plan may be reviewed by the licensing agency
        during the nursing facility’s annual survey and includes:
            a. Program objectives;
            b. Oversight responsibility (e.g., reports to the governing body, QI records);
            c. Nursing facility-wide scope;
            d. Involvement of all resident care disciplines/services;
            e. Includes methods to identify, evaluate, and correct identified problems;
            f. Provides criteria to monitor nursing care and services, including, but not limited
                to:
                      i. Medication administration;
                     ii. Prevention and treatment of decubitus ulcers;
                    iii. Dehydration, and nutritional status and weight loss or gain;
                    iv. Accidents, injuries and unexpected deaths;
                     v. Changes in mental or psychological status;
                    vi. Resident and/or Family Council grievances;
                   vii. Plans of correction developed in response to licensing agency’s
                         inspection reports, and
                  viii. Any other data appropriate to monitor resident’s quality of care and
                         quality of life.
     F. Resident care services, including services rendered by a contractor, are to be evaluated.




Adopted – January 2016
Revised – July 2024

===== PAGE 152 =====
     G. The facility takes and documents appropriate remedial action to address problems
        identified through the quality improvement program. The nursing facility administrator
        takes appropriate remedial actions based on the recommendations of the nursing
        facility’s quality improvement committee. The outcome(s) of the remedial action are
        documented and submitted to the governing body for their consideration.




Adopted – January 2016
Revised – July 2024

               INTERIM UNSECURED LINE OF CREDIT AGREEMENT

       In consideration of any financial accommodation given, or to be given or continued
pursuant to this Interim Unsecured Line of Credit Agreement ("Credit Agreement") by
Quinto Nexgen, LLC a New Jersey limited liability company ("Lender") to Holiday
Operator, LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company ("Borrower"). Borrower hereby
represents, warrants, and agrees as follows, unless Lender waives compliance in writing:

        1. Effective Date. The effective date of this Credit Agreement shall be the date written
on the signature page below ("Effective Day").

        2. Credit Amount. Lender shall loan or make available for Borrower to draw the
principal sum set forth on the signature page below ("Credit Line"). Borrower confirms that any
and all amounts borrowed by Borrower on the Credit Line ("Advances") received on or after
the Effective Date of this Agreement under the Credit Line are subject to the terms of this Credit
Agreement. Any request for an Advance may be made from time to time and in such amounts as
Borrower may choose; provided, however, any requested Advance will not, when added to the
outstanding principal balance of all previous advances, exceed the amount of the Credit Line.

        3. Interest Rate. Any Advances shall be subject to a fixed rate of interest equal to Five
percent (5.00%) per year, subject to adjustment as required under U.S. law to be no less than the
minimum rate of interest as published from time to time by the Internal Revenue Service. Interest
shall accrue in United States Dollars.

        4. Repayment of Credit Line Advances. Borrower shall repay Lender all outstanding
Advances (and all accrued interest on such Advances) under this Credit Line on the last date at
the end of the Term (as defined below). If the date for such payment falls on a Saturday, Sunday,
or holiday, the date for such payment shall be the next following business day after such
Saturday, Sunday or holiday. Should repayment be late, the Advance shall continue to accrue
interest at the rate set forth above. There shall be no prepayment penalty should Borrower repay
the Credit Line and all accrued interest in advance of the term deadline.

       5. Term. All outstanding accrued interest and principal under the Credit Line shall be
due in full on the twelve (12) month anniversary of the Effective Date (the "Term"), unless
terminated sooner as provided herein. The Lender may terminate this Agreement upon thirty (30)
days advance written notice with all outstanding Advances (and all accrued interest on such
Advances) under this Credit Line being due and payable within ninety (90) days of such notice,
whether or not there shall first be an Event of Default hereunder.

       6. Assignment. Lender shall be entitled to assign this Credit Agreement without the
consent of Borrower. Borrower shall not be entitled to assign this Credit Agreement or be
permitted to allow another lender to assume this Credit Agreement without the written consent
of Lender which consent can be withheld by Lender at its sole discretion.

       7. Nature of Business. Borrower operates a skilled nursing facility (the
"Business").

===== PAGE 156 =====
        8. Purpose of Credit. Borrower shall maintain the Business as and in the form presently
conducted and shall utilize the Credit Line only for the following purposes of the Business: (a)
financing the Business; (b) funding of daily operating expenses; and (c) such other matters that
in the opinion of management are necessary to carry out the purposes of the Borrower.

      9. Representations and Warranties; Additional Covenants.

               (a) Borrower is a company duly organized, validly existing and in good standing
under the laws of Rhode Island. Borrower has the lawful power to own its properties and to
engage in the business it conducts, and is duly qualified and in good standing as a corporation in
the jurisdictions wherein the nature of the business transacted by it or property owned by it
makes such qualification necessary.

             (b) Borrower has full power and authority (corporate and other) to borrow the
sums provided for under this Credit Agreement, to execute, deliver, and perform this Credit
Agreement, and all other instruments and agreements made in connection herewith.

              (c) All action by Borrower, its officers, directors, and stockholders, as applicable
that is necessary for the authorization, execution, delivery, and performance of this Credit
Agreement has been duly taken.

               (d) The officers or representatives of Borrower executing this Credit
Agreement are duly and properly in office or acting as representatives and are fully
authorized to execute same.

             (e) This Credit Agreement has been authorized, executed, and delivered by
Borrower, and is a legal, valid, and binding agreement of Borrower, enforceable against it in
accordance with its terms.

              (f) There is no charter, bylaw, or capital stock provision of Borrower, and no
provision of any indenture agreement, or other agreement, written or oral, to which Borrower is
a party or which governs the actions of Borrower or which is otherwise binding upon Borrower
or Borrower's property, nor is there any statute, rule or regulation, or any judgment, decree, or
order of any court or agency binding on Borrower or Borrower's property which would be
contravened by the execution, delivery or performance of this Credit Agreement.

               (g) There is no action, suit, or proceeding at law or in equity or by or before any
governmental instrumentality or other agency now pending, or, to the knowledge of Borrower,
threatened against or affecting Borrower, or any properties or rights of Borrower, which, if
adversely determined, would materially impair the right of Borrower to carry on the Business
substantially as now conducted or would materially adversely affect the financial condition of
Borrower.
